closed:
---
The class, found by execution on the text upstream landed from the fork's offer 1847: a read the hook makes of the pushed objects fails, or returns less than the objects hold, and the hook reads the result as an empty one, so a part-scanned push passes as a clean one. The fork's fix routes every such read through one helper (unscanned: one stderr line naming the ref or the commit and the read, failed_scan=1, the push refused on that flag alone; failed_creds gates the gitleaks advice) or lets the read's own status end the hook; an ABSENT value (an empty target, a tag with no tagger or message, a lightweight tag, a blob or tree tag, a gitlink, a file binary by its bytes, a commit whose diff or message is empty) still reads as empty and passes, and every hit path prints the same text and line numbers as before (the round f4 and f5 contract runs, re-run in r1 and again after the round's audit: byte-identical stderr on the r1 text and its predecessor over 30 shapes, the one difference bash's own null-byte warning naming a moved line). Nine roads are closed in the fold, found one at a time and then by derivation: round f3 the tag fields; round f4 the tip's content grep, its symlink listing, each link's target and the tag TYPE read; round f5 the per-commit added lines, addresses and message; review round r1 the credential scan's read INSIDE gitleaks (the eighth road, below), beside it two holes of the identifier scan's own that the f5 derivation had called closed or not seen (the denylist reader dropped the file's last entry when the file had no trailing newline, every read succeeding and the loop discarding data, the shape that widened the class; and the parent count's failure was reported as the diff-tree's), and, found OPEN by the round's derivation on the r1 fixer's text and closed by the round's audit, the colour road (the ninth, below): a `git log -p` stream the clone's own colour configuration had emptied for the scanner and for the hook's count alike. Review round r2 then found that the colour road was one instance of a CLASS, transforms of a stream both readers shared (six found in two rounds, below), and closed the class structurally rather than transform by transform: the hook's count is derived from the OBJECTS (rev-list, diff-tree --raw, cat-file), which no diff attribute, driver, colour, pretty or date key touches, so a diff-level transform can no longer silence the scan and its check alike; and the premise both scans rest on, that what they read is what the push transfers, is stated at the hook and enforced (replacement off for every git the hook runs, and a refusal under any replace ref, the one shape found where the two diverge). Round r2c then narrowed that refusal to where a scan would run (two arming predicates, identifier_scan_armed and credential_scan_armed, which the scans' own early returns read as well, so the gate and the scans agree by construction: the refusal prevents a false clean report, and where no scan runs there is no report to falsify, so a clone that asked for neither scan stays unaffected, as CLAUDE.md promises) and closed the identifier scan's own instance of the class, a TEXT blob whose path's diff attribute makes git's grep and diff call it binary and skip it, by REFUSAL rather than by scanning (refuse_hidden_paths: the diff attribute of every blob the scan would read, in one git check-attr per ref, and a hidden blob whose bytes are text named with the tip or the commit, the path and the attribute; a blob binary by its bytes stays the documented pass); reading such a blob from the objects is the better long fix, named by the ruling and not taken in this fold. Rounds r2e and r2h (one commit) then took the r2c flags as ruled (the attribute check's scratch directory removed by its exact minted path with the residual stated at the header, the advice naming the attribute as the cause with the explicit-diff remedy and the rename clause) and corrected a premise: log.showRoot set to false, named at r2 and r2c as the one transform no option reaches and so a fail-closed over-refusal, IS reached by an option (`--root`, found by execution in r2e), and since r2h that option in gitleaks_args neutralises the key, so a root commit's diff is scanned and a clean root push passes; the refusal arm stays for a scanner whose git does not honour the option, naming the key, the option and the remedy (the class paragraph below; since r3 the arm states the two facts it observed and names the key as a CANDIDATE cause, since a log shortened for any other reason meets the same two facts). Review round r5 (2026-09-22, romp-manager's round 4 rulings) then found, on the r3g text, that the fail-closed guarantee covered the STATUS-KEYED class alone (a read whose failure returns a non-zero status) and that a second class stood open, the EXIT-0 EMPTY-OR-SHORT-ANSWER class: a tool exiting 0 with nothing, or with less than the objects hold, left paths unread and the push passing (the per-commit changed-path listing answering nothing, the tip's -z listing answering nothing or dropping a record, a rewrite's tr writing nothing, a cat-file printing nothing for a blob with bytes: four shapes, each publishing a hidden text file through a REAL push at the r3g text), where the hook had gated it read by read for the numstats and check-attr alone since round 3; the round gated the CLASS, every such read checked against a second read already in hand and a disagreement refusing the push naming the read and both answers (refuse_hidden_paths since r5: the listing against the paths its verdicts name, the tip's listing against the symlink pass's count and the grep's read list, each rewrite's byte count against its input's with every count validated as digits, the byte judge's zero against the blob's size), the header stating the guarantee per class and the bound of the second (the content reads' own empty answers have no sibling read and stay as absent); found the three arms of rounds r3 to r3f without an executed pin, every case keeping the blob at the pushed tip where the tip-owns rule set it aside before the arm was reached, and gave each a case with the blob ABSENT from the tip, red with the arm disabled; and closed the merge twin and the rename twin of the disclosed binary-to-text shape, the previous version read in each parent of a merge and at a rename's old path so every refusal of that class names its cause and the fetch remedy. Round r5b (2026-09-22, the round 4 lows) then read the added lines through a scratch file rather than a substitution (bash's null-byte warning gone from a real push of this repository's history), printed each advice paragraph only where a refused line named its cause, keyed the two-fact line's tail on the attribute read's outcome, named the path in the three short-read lines, and gave the header's unquoting and a three-parent merge their cases. Review round r6 (2026-09-22, romp-manager's round 5 rulings, group A) then found six more reads of the second class on the r5b text, each shown by a real push with a shim publishing a banned line with nothing printed (a grep exiting 2 over the added lines, a symlink target, an address domain and a message, read as no match; the commit listing answering nothing on a new branch and on a ref update, the per-commit loop running zero times; a parent count of nothing beside an empty combined listing, the merge read as a one-parent commit; a type of nothing over a banned tagger, read as not a tag; an empty symlink target over a blob with bytes, read as an empty target), where round 5 had gated the class read by read and the header had listed the gated reads by hand, and closed BOTH classes BY CONSTRUCTION rather than with six more patches: every read the hook judges goes through one helper, judged_read, which captures the status and the output together and refuses any status outside the read's expected set as unscanned naming the read (the status-keyed class), and every call carries a tag saying how an exit-0 empty answer is judged, gate= where a sibling fact in hand must agree with it or own= where the empty answer is the object's own (the second class); the header's two lists are DERIVED from those tags and pinned by a bats case that reads the tags out of the hook (a name added, dropped or edited by hand, or a call without a tag, is red there), and a read left outside the helper is declared at its site with the reason. Review round r7 (2026-09-23, romp-manager's round 6 rulings, group A) then found, on the r6b text and by real pushes with a tool exiting 0 and printing nothing, that round 5's ruling that the exit-0 class was closed by construction held for the gate= reads alone: five own= sites and the ref list itself still published (the MESSAGE grep over a commit's subject and a tag's message, under a silent grep and under the REAL grep over a byte that is no UTF-8 in a UTF-8 locale; the TIP CANDIDATES join under a silent awk and a silent tr; the JOIN of a commit's verdicts under either; the ADDED LINES diff under a silent awk; a tag's TAGGER field and MESSAGE under an awk silent for their programs, and a commit's MESSAGE under a git silent on its format; every ref of a push under a silent cat), a capture file the helper could not open published with bash's own line the only sign, and the ADDED LINES grep's exit-0 silence was refused with the sort named as the cause: thirteen publications in one round, none printing a hook line. Round r7 closed them (the class paragraphs and the rows below): the MESSAGE grep refuses a match that printed no hit line, reads with -a and is gate=; tip_candidates is gated on a count in hand, the -z listing's regular files with bytes less the grep's read list, against the rows it appended, counted by their NUL terminators in the shell (nul_records); join_candidates and added_lines each record the count of rows they printed in a count file the caller compares with the rows appended or the capture's line count; the tag object is read ONCE through judged_read -o, judged against its size (when the capture was empty, until round 8; since round 8 every capture's byte count against the object's size), and its tagger, message and object line are parsed by the shell (tag_field, tag_message and tag_unread retired); a commit's MESSAGE log asks for a marker line ahead of the message and is judged against it; the ref list is read by the shell's own builtin with no tool between git and the loops; judged_read opens its -o or -a file once to a descriptor before the command and refuses a failed open whatever the expected set; the ADDED LINES grep's empty hits under exit 0 are refused before the sort runs; and the header's own= paragraph became the DERIVED BOUND, naming what stays own= and why a tool silent there publishes nothing by itself (no own= read is a read of an object's content since). Round r8 closed each with its real-push case, red at 05d7f837b by publication: the diff led by the commit's own sha (--always in place of --no-commit-id), a marker the awk requires before it counts, a diff that never prints it refused by its own arm naming the git read (case 208); the match made a comparison by the shell with no tool (identity cases 41 and 42); every tag capture's byte count against the object's size (message case 28); the tree's size read apart beside two empty listings (case 211); the ref list read before any tool runs (case 210); and with them the message log's tail marker (message case 27) and the tag header ended at a BLANK line as the retired awk's !NF ended it (message case 29), each red at 05d7f837b by publication too, and the scratch directory removed from an EXIT trap (case 212, red there on the directory left behind). Ruling A.8 then made the POPULATION a predicate: tests/pre-push-reads.tsv holds every tool read of the hook (each judged_read tag, each own= read and each read the population census finds outside the helper), one row per read with the fact that bounds its answer's START and its END or the reason an empty or cut-short exit-0 answer is the safe side, a case that drives the read with its tool silent and a case that drives it with its answer cut short (or the reason no cut applies), each through a real push; the header's gate= and own= block is generated from it by tests/pre-push-reads-header.sh and the table case (tests/pre-push-hook.bats case 227) pins the block, the rows and the cases, so no list of reads is written by hand and the population is judged by the table's rule, no longer listed by tag. Driving the table found more, each closed with its case red at the previous hook through a real push, by publication unless said: round r8b's four loops that ran tools while reading their lists on stdin (the two ref loops, the symlink loop and the byte judge's entries loop, each now on a descriptor of its own, where a tool that read its stdin took the rest of the list and a later ref, link or entry went unscanned; cases 222 to 225, three by publication and the symlink loop's, 224, refused there only on the entry count with its banned target unread); round r8b2's ANCESTRY read (merge-base --is-ancestor answers by its status alone, so a silent exit 0 was the ancestor verdict and a middle commit's banned line published; the answer of git merge-base must now be the pushed commit itself, since round r9a the commit the pushed object peels to, read apart as a whole commit name; case 236); round r8b3's mechanism, that a fact bounding an answer's START says nothing of its END, and its nine closures, each of an answer with exit 0 that published, cut short or, for the ERR line, silent: the ADDED LINES diff's tail marker (the commit given again on --stdin as its own parent; case 304), the ADDRESSES log's tail marker (case 308), the COMMIT COUNT read apart against the commit listing (cases 297 and 298), a symlink target's byte count against its size (case 295), the symlink listing's whole entries (case 293), the -z listing's whole-entry count (case 315), the REPOSITORY ROOT's line after the path (case 332), the CHOSEN ADDRESSES' newline terminator (case 338), and the scanner log's ERR line read as a word answer and made a gate row (case 336), beside two hardenings with no publication behind them (the ancestry's answer a whole name; the empty tree's name as long as the commit's own); round r8b4's case for the COMMIT listing's whole-commit-name arm, a third hardening (case 353, red under the r8b4 audit's mutant of the arm); and round r8b5's short case for the parent count read for the report (case 354, red under the r8b5 audit's mutant that reads the count whole; the cut changes the report and never the verdict). These were found by the fold owner's own rounds r8b to r8b5, after round 7's ruling. A.8's last sentence reserves a read found publishing while its row said safe for the repository owner's design decision, and round 8b3 found two: the ERR line (an own= read whose row said safe, which published a credential beside a scanner that counted a commit whole with its credential file unread) and the chosen addresses (an own= row whose row said safe, which published under an answer cut to a prefix of one address, a commit stamped under a banned domain), so the closures of rounds r8b to r8b5 were the proposal pending that ruling, which round 9's decision settled (below). Review round r9 (romp-manager's round 8 rulings, 2026-09-24) then found the credential scan's coverage check whole at the granularity of commits and no finer: gitleaks ran git log -p itself and counted a commit once one of its files had a hunk, so a stream cut inside a commit with nothing on stderr passed both of the hook's arms whatever git's exit status, and the round 8 refuters published a credential through real pushes that way, by a faulty git on PATH and by a real git killed partway (ruling A.1, extra5-1). The repository owner's decision took option (b) of the three ruling A.2 put (romp-manager for the owner, 2026-09-24), and flags 87, 88 and 89, whose texts routed round 8b's publications to that decision (the four stdin loops, the ancestry read, round 8b3's nine closures with the ERR line), were accepted with it, so those closures stand as built. Round r9d built option (b) under romp-manager's three rulings on the design (2026-09-24): the credential scan now reads the pushed changes itself and hands gitleaks exactly those bytes, and gitleaks runs no git, so the read inside gitleaks that round 8 found publishing is closed by construction rather than disclosed (the property paragraph and item 33 below). The new read has its row in the table, the CREDENTIAL FEED of the push, with a silent case (268) and a short case, the refuters' within-commit cut, red at cad898dd2 by publication (332; ruling A.3 (2)). The round's other findings were built meanwhile by the fold owner's rounds r9a, r9b and r9bc, each with its case through a real push: the ANCESTRY read compared with the commit the pushed object peels to, read apart as a gate row of its own, where three annotated-tag updates had been refused with a false cause (cases 355 to 360); the tip's SIZE refusal naming both causes, a tree of empty directories alone a disclosed residual (case 361); the tag remedy keyed per cause, not per push (cases 362 to 365); the newline test read by the shell, its tool and the table's NEWLINE COUNT row gone (cases 366 and 367); the loop census counting judged_read and the hook's own functions, the addresses loop moved to descriptor 7 (case 226); the population census reading the command word raw and unquoted at its offset and more shapes besides, pinning others absent and disclosing five it neither reads nor pins, each with a witness (case 203; the five then known, not a complete list: round r10c derived the list from bash's expansions of a command word, item 34); the table predicate requiring each case to drive its own read and reading the generated block from the first BEGIN marker alone (case 227); and two rows' text made true (the chosen addresses named beside the ERR line; the -z listing cut after its tab, case 368). Round r9d retired the reads the old credential scan needed and re-aimed their case slots in place (hook cases 70 to 76, 173, 185, 268 to 270, 280, 281, 290, 291, 332 to 334, 341, 349 and 350), each title naming the read the slot held (R5), so a case number this entry cites for a retired read is the r9b text's. Review round r10 (romp-manager's round 9 rulings, 2026-09-25) then found the credential feed's awk passing silently every line it did not read, and a merge's combined diff applying git's binary verdict whatever --text says (combine-diff.c computes it without reading the text flag): for a path git calls binary in the merge's result or in any parent it printed Binary files differ and no hunk, and a credential in the merge's own lines there published with every count agreeing, for each of five causes; a merge of 64 or more parents published the same way (ruling A, correctness-1). Rounds r10a to r10c built the rulings (item 34): the feed refuses a line it cannot read (credential_feed); a merge's binary path is read whole from its result blob (merge_binary_reads, blob_pieces), at the cost that a merge whose binary path's whole blob holds a credential a remote already published refuses the push; a commit of 64 or more parents refuses the push in both scans (parent_counts, and the identifier scan's PARENT COUNT); the scanner must be gitleaks 8.25.0 or later, its version read where a scan would run (scanner_version); the path-scoped copies take a fixed stem and their directories are made in bounded batches; and the population census's disclosed shapes are derived from bash's expansions of a command word. Review round r11 (romp-manager's round 10 rulings, 2026-09-26) then found, by execution under gitleaks 8.30.1 and 8.28.0, that round 10b's fixed copy names let a path-keyed element of the config the additive run reads drop a path-scoped rule's finding in the copy while the byte figure agreed (a per-rule or targeted path allowlist, in either condition, or a rule's own path, matching the copy's name and not the file's path), so a credential main's hook refuses published (ruling A, extra6-1); that a path the config gives a rule the additive run does not enable was evaluated against a piece's number and never fired (S1 to S4, the coordinator's decision 6); and that a relative [extend] path refused every clean push with content (decision 5); beside them eight refusal arms no case drove, the feed's diff and three format reads open to environment and configuration they did not pin, directory batches that ignored the environment's share of the argument floor, a cut size answer refused naming the content read, G4 and the loop census checking less than their titles said, a test span starting ten words into the text it holds equal, and records naming no witness (groups B to I). Round r10e and rounds r11a to r11c2 built the rulings (item 35): a repository config whose rules carry a path condition refuses the push, read by the hook's own reader before either scan (gitleaks_config); every scanner run is handed the reader's copy of the config, a relative [extend] or GITLEAKS_CONFIG made absolute; a third run probes each copy's name under the five path-scoped rules and scans a second copy of each selected piece at its root (probe_check, credential_line); the feed's diff and the format reads pin their inputs; the per-piece directories are made by xargs (piece_dirs); and every arm has its case. One offer for the whole class with the harness; the offer is authorised and sequenced after the fork landing (the closing paragraph).

The eighth road, stated as executed (the round f5 entry stated it too narrowly, as gitleaks reading its git log FAILURE as an empty scan, and a fix built to that spec would still have failed open): gitleaks 8.30.1 (and 8.28.0, the version CI pins) runs `git -C <root> log -p -U0 <range> --diff-merges=first-parent` itself, and aborts its scan on the FIRST stderr line from that git that is not one it allowlists as a rename-detection warning (by execution: the two rename-detection warnings and the `diff.renameLimit` hint pass; the source names the auto-gc notice too), WHATEVER git's exit status: it logs `ERR [git] <line>` and `ERR error="stderr is not empty"`, drops the rest of the scan, reports the commits it did read as `N commits scanned`, says `no leaks found` for every finding not already emitted, and exits 0. The commit count and byte count it prints may still be the full expected ones (three commits parsed, the noise on the oldest, `3 commits scanned` equal to `git rev-list --count`, a live token in the range, `no leaks found`), so neither git's exit status nor an agreeing commit count distinguishes an aborted scan from a clean one. The hook at f5 classified on the exit status alone (`case $rc in 0) ;;`), so exit 0 read as CLEAN and the credential published, with nothing printed but gitleaks' own lines. The road is open on every clone, the maintainer's included: with the denylist armed, no read of the identifier arm fails in these modes (`git diff-tree` is plumbing and applies no textconv; `git grep` reads the raw blobs), so the f5 entry's sentence that the denylist masked the road was false. Since round r9d the road is closed by construction: gitleaks scans files the hook wrote from the lines the pushed commits add (and, since round r10a, from a merge's binary path read whole from its result blob; since round r11b a third run also reads probe texts of the hook's own beside second copies of the path-scoped pieces) and runs no git (items 33 to 35 below). The executed fault modes, each a REAL push through the installed hook with the denylist armed and a credential assembled at run time, each publishing at the f5 text with zero hook lines:

- a git that writes ONE benign line to stderr and otherwise runs unchanged (a shim on gitleaks' invocation shape; romp-manager's own reproduction): `ERR [git] ...`, `1 commits scanned` of two, `no leaks found`, exit 0; two live credentials published.
- a content-preserving textconv that exits 0 and writes one stderr line (`.gitattributes` `diff=<name>` plus `diff.<name>.textconv`; a personal `core.attributesFile` reaches it too): git log -p exits 0; gitleaks reports the FULL count (1 of 1, 2 of 2, 3 of 3, agreeing with `git rev-list --count` every time), `no leaks found`, exit 0; the token on the remote. This is the mode that defeats a readability probe (`git log -p ... >/dev/null || unscanned` passes: git exited 0) and a count comparison alone.
- a textconv whose converter is missing: git log -p exits 128 (`fatal: cannot exec`), `git diff-tree` exits 0 and `git grep` 1 for the identifier arm; gitleaks `0 commits scanned`, exit 0; published.
- a git that rejects `--diff-merges=first-parent` (any git below 2.31): `ERR [git] error: unknown option`, `0 commits scanned`, exit 0; published, every identifier-arm read succeeding.
- a merge whose side branch the remote already holds, the credential in the plain commit before the merge, the side branch's dropped file's blob unlinked from the store: `git grep` 1, `git ls-tree` 0, `git diff-tree` 0 on both new commits (the merge's combined diff is empty), gitleaks' git log -p 128 (`unable to read`), `1 commits scanned`, exit 0; published. (The f5 entry's shape, the added file's blob gone from the tip's own commit, makes diff-tree exit 128 and the f5 ADDED LINES read refuses first; the shape matters, and this one is the reachable one.)
- a log that comes up short with no stderr line at all (gitleaks' git given `--max-count=1`, the secret in the older of two commits): `1 commits scanned`, `no leaks found`, exit 0; published. No ERR line is written in this mode; only the count condition sees it.
- a git that exits 128 inside gitleaks (`fatal: bad object`), and the f5 entry's fallback shape (a range this clone resolves whose walk then fails): `ERR [git] fatal: ...`, `0 commits scanned`, exit 0; published.
- the controls: two live secrets with a healthy git are refused as a finding (exit 2, both rules named, redacted); git's rename-limit warnings (`diff.renameLimit=1` over twelve renames) are logged at WRN and the finding is still reported, exit 2.
- not executed, by assessment: a transient fault inside gitleaks' own process (a signal, EMFILE) is not reproducible by a separate probe of git and is visible only in gitleaks' own log, which is why the remedy reads that log rather than probing git again.

The fix, in the fold (review round r1; the rule: stop inferring "clean" from exit 0, which says gitleaks reported no finding, not that it scanned; require positive evidence of coverage and refuse without it). The mechanism this paragraph records held from r1 to r9b and was retired in round r9d, when the hook took gitleaks' read into its own (the property paragraph; item 33): the rule stands, and its positive evidence is since then gitleaks' byte figure against the bytes the hook wrote. The hook captures gitleaks' stderr, its log, apart from its stdout (the findings still stream live to the hook's stderr through a third descriptor: `log=$( { "$gl" git ... 2>&1 1>&3; } 3>&2 ) || rc=$?`), re-emits the log, keeps the exit-status arms (1 or above 2: failed_scan and failed_creds as before; 2: a finding), and when gitleaks CLAIMS a scan (exit 0 or 2) applies two independent conditions to an SGR-stripped copy of the log: (1) any line at its ERR level refuses, `the CREDENTIAL scan of <ref> (<sha10>) did not complete: gitleaks logged an error (<the first ERR line>)`, failed_creds=1; (2) the reported `<n> commits scanned.` must equal the hook's own count of the commits with content to scan, `covered <reported> of the <expected> commits with content to scan` on a mismatch, `reported no commit count, so what it read is unknown` when the line is absent, failed_creds=1 on each. Two conditions because either alone has a hole: the short mode writes no ERR line, and an aborted scan can report the whole count; together they hold the scan whole at the granularity of commits and no finer (round 8's refuters: a stream cut inside a commit with nothing on stderr passes both, the property paragraph below). At the r1 text the expected count was derived by a new helper, scannable_commits, over the SAME `git log -p -U0 --format='commit %H' --diff-merges=first-parent` stream by gitleaks' own rule (a commit counts once a file that is neither deleted nor binary has a hunk; gitleaks' sources for v8.30.1 and v8.28.0 read: one `addCommit` per fragment, a fragment one hunk of a non-deleted non-binary file, the count the distinct shas), because `git rev-list --count` over-counts every empty, delete-only, binary-only, mode-only or rename-only commit and every merge with an empty first-parent diff (nine of 22 shapes disagree with gitleaks; that derivation agreed with both gitleaks versions on all 22 and on a 4556-commit range of the fork's own history, 4544 both). The same stream, but not the same bytes: the r1 audit had both reads ask git for a plain stream, `git log --no-color` in scannable_commits and `--no-color` appended to gitleaks' `--log-opts`, because a transform the clone's configuration applies to the stream reaches both readers alike and a count that agrees with an emptied scan is no evidence (the colour road, below). Review round r2 found five more transforms of that stream and replaced the derivation: scannable_commits now reads the OBJECTS and consults no stream (the class paragraph below), and gitleaks' options live in one place, gitleaks_args. The derivation's own failure is its own road, read before the scan: `expected=$(scannable_commits $range) || { rc=$?; expected=""; }` with the clause `the COMMITS of <ref> (<sha10>) could not be counted for the credential scan (a git read exited N)` (`git log exited N` at r1), failed_creds=1; since r6 the read goes through judged_read (gate: the COUNT of commits with content to scan, the same clause with {rc} the status) and its answer is judged as a run of digits, an exit-0 answer of any other shape refused as not a count (the count's awk answering nothing beside a scanner handed a shorter log PASSED at the r5b text, the comparison skipped on the empty count: bats case 185 with the real scanner, red there), as are the repository root (3021 at the r9b text; 2941 at r8c; 2405 at r7b), the range probe (3057; 2977; 2433) and the three reads of the scanner's log (3120, 3125, 3138; 3040, 3045, 3058; 2486, 2491, 2500), each with the scanner's advice on a refusal where the r5b text ended the hook by errexit with no line. The rev-list probe's comment now says what it covers (an UNRESOLVABLE range only; what gitleaks did with the range is read from its log, not its exit status), and the hook's header paragraph states the road, the two-condition remedy and the colour road. The invocation gained that one argument at r1, `--no-color` after `--diff-merges=first-parent` inside `--log-opts` (nine plain-stream options since r2 and ten since r2h, `--root` the tenth, built by gitleaks_args); tests/install-sh.bats's pins on the invocation are substring globs and still match (its stub strips the `--log-opts` value after the range and `--diff-merges=first-parent`), and its stub scanner now reports the range's commit count in gitleaks' own line shape, since the hook requires the count as positive evidence (the harness paragraph). Verified by execution with gitleaks 8.30.1 and 8.28.0, the hook fed its ref line directly and through REAL pushes: the noise mode is refused on both conditions (the ERR line, and `covered 0 of the 2` or `1 of the 2`: the abort's count varies with the race between gitleaks' two readers, and both refuse), the short mode by the count alone (`covered 1 of the 2`), the bad-object and fallback modes by both, the rename warning and the two-secret control still as a finding, the remote unchanged in every refusal; a clean push prints byte-identical output to the f5 text's; on a finding one INF line moves (the log is shown after the live findings). In tests/pre-push-hook.bats, the section "the credential scan's own read: gitleaks' log" (the real scanner, skipped where gitleaks is absent as tests/gitleaks-config.bats skips; the probe tokens assembled at run time; a second generic shim, `git_passing_through <bash test over "$@"> <stderr line or empty> [<appended argument>]`, keyed on gitleaks' invocation shape and no read of the hook's own, with the wrappers gitleaks_git_noisy, gitleaks_git_short and gitleaks_git_warning): the two-secret control, a clean pass with the count shown as before, the no-content shapes (at r1 an empty commit, a deletion, a binary add: four commits by rev-list, `1 commits scanned`, no hook line: a pin on the derivation rule, red on a hook that counted with rev-list; re-cut at r2 for the resolved invocation, under which a binary add and a pure rename count as their bytes say: seven commits, three counted), the ERR mode and the short mode (each red at the f5 text at `[ "$status" -eq 1 ]`: the fail-open), the allowlisted rename warning as a WRN pass control, and the two colour cases (below). Round r3 added, in the same section, the count's two remaining arms with the REAL scanner (a blob the size read cannot find fails the count with the exact clause `a git read exited 3`, where under ROMP_NO_GITLEAKS the count never runs and with the scanner a looser assertion stays green through gitleaks' own ERR arm; an ADDED gitlink whose commit this store lacks passes, counted by both, pinning the `$2 != "160000"` filter of the sizes read, without which the gitlink's absent commit reads `missing` and a clean submodule push is refused; bats cases 46 and 47, red under mutants m10 and m11), and in the showRoot section the one case that holds WHERE the count comes from (round r3's ruling C): a git shim stripping `--root` from every invocation carrying the word `log` and from no diff-tree (git_stripping_root_from_log), log.showRoot false, a credential in the root commit, asserting status 1, `covered 1 of the 2 commits with content to scan` and the key line (case 74); a count derived from an equally hardened `git log -p` stream, the shape round r2's ruling excluded, agrees with the shortened scan and passes it (exit 0, the credential published: mutant m12, red on case 74 alone, where 87 of the 88 cases at 89589e7d3 stayed green on that mutant), since every plain-stream option that neutralises a key for the scanner's git neutralises it for such a count alike, so the thirteen transform cases hold that each named transform is closed and not the derivation; the file's header, the transform section's comment and the count-read case's comment (case 54 since r3, r1's 52: its shim keys on the count read's own shape, `diff-tree --stdin`, not on the derivation) say so since r3.

The property in one sentence: no read the hook itself makes of the pushed objects can fail or come up short in a way the hook cannot tell from an empty result, because each read's status is captured before its output is judged, a non-zero status refuses the push as unscanned, and an exit-0 answer is judged against a fact in hand or is the object's own (the two classes below); there is no read inside gitleaks since round r9d: the credential scan reads the pushed changes itself (list_commits, the listing and its gates shared with the identifier scan; one git diff-tree --stdin over the whole push, the credential feed, each commit's head and tail markers checked in the order fed and the commits read whole counted against the commits fed; the added lines written to pieces of at most 98,304 bytes, each opened by a ~ line; since round r10a every line between a commit's two names read, recognized as carrying nothing the feed needs, or routed, and any other refused as a line the feed cannot read (credential_feed); a merge's combined diff applies git's binary verdict whatever --text says, so its Binary files differ notice is routed and the merge's own version of that path read whole from its result blob, pieced as the added lines are (merge_binary_reads, blob_pieces), at the cost that a merge whose binary path's whole blob holds a credential a remote already published refuses the push (its witness the round 10b case in tests/pre-push-hook.bats titled "round 10b (A.2's disclosed cost, with its witness)"); a commit of 64 or more parents refused by name in both scans, since git's combined diff drops or garbles the lines such a merge adds (parent_counts, and the identifier scan's PARENT COUNT); and since round r10b a gitleaks older than 8.25.0, or one whose version answer is not a dotted version, refused where a scan would run (scanner_version); since round r11a the config every run is handed read first by the hook's own reader where a scan would run, a repository config whose rules carry a path condition refused before either run (gitleaks_config); and since round r11c the feed's diff-tree run with GIT_DIFF_OPTS unset and diff.suppressBlankEmpty=false, and parent_counts' rev-list with its output encoding pinned (credential_feed, parent_counts)), gitleaks scans those pieces in directory mode and runs no git, its one byte figure is compared with the bytes the hook wrote, any line at its ERR level refuses, and each finding is named with its commit and file from the piece's name, so the stream cut inside a commit through which round 8's refuters published a credential (a faulty git on PATH, a real git killed partway; gitleaks 8.30.1 and 8.28.0) is now a cut of the hook's own feed, refused naming it (the table's CREDENTIAL FEED row, whose short case is that cut, red at cad898dd2 by publication), where until r9d (afa552298, which retired them) the two arms judging gitleaks' own git log held the scan whole at the granularity of commits and no finer; the repository owner chose this, option (b) (romp-manager for the owner, 2026-09-24), and romp-manager's three rulings on the design set its shape (item 33: the five path-scoped rules kept by an additive run whose figure is checked the same way, a merge read by the lines in none of its parents and a rename by its edits, and directory mode, which gives up gitleaks' built-in path exemptions; item 35: a per-rule or targeted path allowlist, in either condition, or a rule's own path, that treats a copy's name differently from the file's path changes that rule's verdict in the copy without skipping it, so the figure agrees, and since round r11b a third run, the probe run, probes each copy's name under the five rules and scans a second copy of each selected piece at its root, refusing when a probe is not named under its rule or a second copy holds a finding; a global path allowlist that matches a copy's name skips it and refuses on the figure); and the objects both scans read are the ones the push transfers, replacement off, or the push is refused before either scan (where a scan runs at all: a clone that asked for neither makes no report a replace ref could falsify); and a blob the identifier scan's two content reads SKIPPED as binary, by git's own verdict on each read (the tip's grep asked what it read; the per-commit diff's numstat over the pair it reads, or for a merge its combined patch itself), whose bytes are text, is refused rather than read as empty, whatever rule made git call it binary (its path's diff attribute, core.bigFileThreshold, a driver under any name, a rule a later git adds), less a blob the pushed tip's tree holds, which the tip half judges. The guarantee is of two KINDS, stated per class at the hook's header since r5 and closed BY CONSTRUCTION since r6: every read the hook judges (every git whose output a check reads, every grep over captured content, every tool a join runs: 63 call lines carrying 59 distinct tags since r11c, where the DIRECTORIES of the path-scoped copies' tag came in; 62 and 58 at r11b, where the PROBE CHECK of the path-scoped copies' came in; 61 and 57 at r11a, where the BYTE COUNT of a gitleaks config file's came in; 60 and 56 from r10b to r10e, where the VERSION of the scanner's came in; 59 and 55 at r10a, where the PARENT COUNTS', the RESULT listing's, the SIZE of a merge's binary blob's and the CONTENT of a merge's binary blob's came in; 55 and 51 from r9d to r9e, where the CREDENTIAL FEED's and the BYTE COUNT line's tags came in and the repository root's, the count's, the colour strip's, the COMMIT COUNT line's and the range probe's went; 58 and 54 from r8b3 to r9b, the peel's tag in and the NEWLINE COUNT's out at r9a; 56 and 52 at r7b, 53 and 49 at r6b) goes through one helper, judged_read, which captures the status and the output together (read_rc and read_out, or a file for a NUL-delimited listing and for the added lines, which a substitution would mangle) and refuses any status outside the read's EXPECTED set as unscanned naming the read, {rc} in the caller's clause replaced by the status (a grep: 0 and 1; a git: 0; the set stated at each call, with the reason where it is wider: 0 and 128 for the symlink listing, whose 128 is a pushed object that peels to a blob, and for the commit listing (and until r9d the range probe), whose 128 is a range this clone cannot resolve; 0, 3 and 4 for the join, 3 and 4 its short-read codes), the STATUS-KEYED class; and the EXIT-0 EMPTY-OR-SHORT-ANSWER class, a read that exits 0 with an empty or a short answer the check would read as nothing to judge, which has no status to read and is answered at each call site by the TAG the helper is given: gate= where a second read or fact already in hand must agree with the answer, named at the call site, a disagreement refusing the push as unscanned naming the read and both answers (52 names since r11c, 51 at r11b, 50 at r11a, 49 from r10b to r10e, 48 at r10a, 44 from r9d to r9e, 46 from r8b3 to r9b, 42 at r7b and 32 at r6b: the content grep of the tip against its own status, a match that printed no hit line being short; the symlink listing against the peeled type; a symlink target read as empty against the blob's size, since r8b3 every target's byte count against it; the commit listing answering nothing against the peeled type, else against the remote-tracking refs containing the pushed commit OR the pushed commit's ancestry over the remote's (since r8b2 the answer of git merge-base, which must be the commit the pushed object peels to, since r9a read apart and judged a whole commit name before the comparison, the pushed commit itself until then; since r8b3 a non-empty listing against the COMMIT COUNT of the same range, read apart); the parent count against the commit named first; the addresses log against its two role lines and, since r8b3, the tail marker after them; a type against the object types; a tag's object field against its type; the -z listing against the symlink pass's entry count and the grep's read list, since r8b3 its whole entries counted and each line of the symlink listing a whole entry; each rewrite's byte count against its input's, every count a run of digits; the changed-path listing against the paths its verdicts name; the combined patch, its section rows, the per-parent listing, the rename candidates, the numstats and the empty tree's name against the join, where a post-image no verdict answers for is a short read; the byte judge's count of zero against the blob's size; the sort's answer against the lines it was given; until r9d the repository root and the count of commits with content against their shape, the root since r8b3 by the tail line true after it, and the scanner log's colour strip and count line against the count arm; since r9d the credential feed against each commit's head marker in the order fed, its tail marker after the commit's diff and the count of commits fed, and the scanner log's byte figure against the bytes the feed's awk wrote; since r10a the parent counts against each commit's header and its name again after its parents, in the order given, and against the count fed, a merge's result listing against the path its combined section named, exactly one record, a merge's binary blob's size, a run of digits, and its content read against that size; since r10b the scanner's version against the shape of a dotted version and the floor; since r11a a gitleaks config file's byte count against the bytes the shell read of it, line by line; since r11b the probe check's answer, a line per selected piece, against its closing line and the probes written; since r11c the per-piece directories against a test of each directory xargs was given; since r7 the message grep and the added-lines grep against their own status, a match that printed no hit line being short; the tip candidates join against the -z listing's regular files with bytes less the grep's read list, the file count and the line count read apart and each a run of digits; the join of a commit's verdicts against the count its awk recorded of the rows it printed; the added-lines diff against its awk's count and the capture's line count, since r8 against the head marker --always prints ahead of the diff and since r8b3 against the tail marker its second --stdin line prints after it; the message log against the marker line its format asks for ahead of the message and, since r8, the tail marker after it; the tag object, read once, against its size, when the capture was empty until r8 and since r8 by every capture's byte count against the object's size, and its object line's presence against the type read; since r8 the tip's symlink listing answering nothing against the SIZE of the tip's tree; since r8b3 the scanner log's error line against the word its awk must answer, none or an error line; each such fact stated with the fact that bounds the answer's END since r8b3, the table's rule), or own= where the empty answer is the object's own and no sibling fact exists, so it passes as absent (7 names since r9d, 8 from r8b3 to r9b, 10 at r7b and 17 at r6b: the address-domain grep, since r7b the chosen-addresses read, the symlink-target grep, the replace-refs listing, the tip listing join against the read list, the tip blobs and the listing join of a commit against its verdicts, and until r9d the range probe, retired with the scan it served; the chosen-address match left the list in r8, a comparison by the shell with no tool since, and the scanner log's ERR line in r8b3, gate= since; the seven that left the list in r7 are gate= or retired: the added-lines diff and its grep, the message log and its grep, the tip candidates join, the join of a commit's verdicts, a tag's tagger field and message). The header's two lists are DERIVED from those tags, not written by hand: tests/pre-push-hook.bats case 174 reads every tag out of the hook and fails when either list names anything else, misses a tag, or a call carries none (red under four header mutants: a name dropped from the own list, a name nobody tags added, a gate name edited by hand, a call site stripped of its tag; since r7b red too with the chosen-addresses tag removed from the own= list; since r8b2 the lines under each name are generated too, the read's fact beside it, and case 174 skips those fact lines, R4), since r8b the two lists and since r8b2 the header's bound and own= paragraphs beside them are GENERATED from tests/pre-push-reads.tsv by tests/pre-push-reads-header.sh (the table case, 227, red when the block differs from the generator's output by a byte, when a tag has no row or a row no tag, when a read the census finds outside the helper is matched by no row's key or by two, and when a row names no case for either drive, and since r9b when a row's case or short case is another read's, its title carrying no `: <read>:`, and when the block is read from any BEGIN marker but the first), and since r7b case 203 pins the POPULATION half: a census over the hook's text finds no command line running a reading tool (the vocabulary stated in the case as its bound) that is not a judged_read call, inside a function the helper is handed whole, an array-literal assignment or under an `outside judged_read:` marker, quoted text masked quote-aware so code inside a command substitution stays visible, `|| true` and `|| :` inside a passed-whole body listed as a second bound (red on a planted `tipname=$(git rev-parse ...)` in the ref loop, the round 6 refuters' plant, naming the planted line, and on its double-quoted spelling, the second refuter's bypass; green at 47b595b26 and at the r7b head, 28 command lines read, 17 in passed bodies, 10 under markers, 1 array literal, 0 undeclared, and at the r8b5 and r8c heads the same with 58 call sites, and at the r9b head 58 call sites with 27 command lines read and 16 in passed bodies, newline_count's pipeline retired with the newline test's tool, and at the r9d head 55 call sites, 22 command lines read, 14 in passed bodies (credential_feed's among them), 8 under markers, no array literal (gitleaks_args names no git since r9d) and 0 undeclared, and at the r10b2 head 60 call sites, 27 command lines read, 19 in passed bodies (blob_pieces' and parent_counts' among them), 8 under markers, no array literal and 0 undeclared, and at the r11c2 head 63 call sites, 30 command lines read, 22 in passed bodies (config_bytes', probe_check's and piece_dirs' among them), 8 under markers, no array literal and 0 undeclared; since r8b, ruling B, the census reads a backtick substitution in the code and inside double quotes, the text after a call's `--` through the same splitter, a command word by its basename with a leading backslash dropped and past a prefix's option words, and command -v and -V as lookups that run nothing, each shape planted in the case and flagged once; since r9b (ruling F; the round 8 refuters found more shapes it neither read nor pinned than the two its bound comment then named, a variable-named command word and a read inside an eval'd string) it splits at a lone & and at |&, shifts a lone redirection's operand, reads the command word back from the RAW text at its offset in the masked text, unquoted and by its basename (since round r9bc a word whose quoting or escaping on the same line splits the tool's name as well, gi\t, g''it and "g"it; a backslash-newline splits the name too and is not read, ruling J), and counts a command word it does not know as a read of a reading tool's word later in its simple command (fail-closed; no such line in the hook); it pins these shapes ABSENT from the hook's comment-stripped raw text (a command word beginning with a parameter expansion or a substitution other than "$@", "$detail" and "$gl"; a variable assigned a reading tool's name or path, or a default naming one; a substitution whose first word is a lookup naming a reading tool, the scanner's own lookup excepted by its exact text; an eval; a here-doc; a trap action holding a tool's word; source or . of a substitution; bash -c or sh -c); and since round r9bc shapes it neither reads nor pins are DISCLOSED in CENSUS_TOOLS' bound comment, each with a witness in the case that passes both, five at round r9bc (a variable command word under a wrapper it does not know; a command word holding an expansion that does not begin it; a command after a string continued from the line before; an ANSI-C quoted word; env -S with a command string), a list found shape by shape and not complete, and since round r10c derived from bash's expansions of a command word (brace, tilde, parameter, command substitution, arithmetic, process substitution, word splitting, pathname expansion and quote removal, with alias expansion and line continuation), each stated in the bound comment as read, pinned absent or disclosed with a witness, the disclosed count following from that derivation (ruling J, extra7-4: eight, the five with brace expansion, pathname expansion and a backslash-newline inside a command word), the hook holding none). And since r8b case 226 holds every loop that runs a tool to a descriptor of its own, since r9b counting a judged_read call and a call to a function of the hook whose body runs a tool, resolved transitively, as a tool run, and only -u with a descriptor from 1 to 9 as a descriptor (ruling C: the census had counted a tool only as a command word on the loop's own lines, and two of round 8b's four loops ran theirs through judged_read or a function; six descriptor loops at the r9b head, the addresses loop's on descriptor 7 new, and ten at the r9d head, four new on descriptor 6 over the credential feed's index, its path-scoped index twice and a scanner report, none of them running a tool, and eleven from the r10a head through round r10e, merge_binary_reads' loop over a merge's routed binary paths new on descriptor 6, its body running judged_read, and twelve since the r11a head, gitleaks_config's read of a config file's lines new on descriptor 7, running no tool; since round r11c, ruling G, the census reads for loops in both forms and select loops beside while and until, a for loop flagged when its own stdin is a list and a body read takes that stdin, a select loop whenever its body runs a tool, one plant per form in the case). The second class's BOUND is the own= list: a tool that exits 0 while printing nothing at one of those own= reads (seventeen at r6b, ten at r7b, eight from r8b3 to r9b, seven since r9d) is outside the guarantee by design, since no sibling fact exists to check it against (round 5 had listed the gated reads by hand and named four content reads as the bound, the r5 hook fixer's FLAG 2 and the r5 audit's finding 3; round 6's refuters found six reads of the class outside that list, each publishing through a real push, which is why the list is derived now; the residual paragraph names the five joins among the seventeen at r6b and the three among the ten at r7b, the eight from r8b3 and the seven since r9d). Since r7 the own= list is the DERIVED BOUND stated at the header: no own= read is a read of an object's CONTENT (round 6's refuters found five own= reads and the ref list publishing under a tool exiting 0 printing nothing, each by a real push, thirteen publications, and round 7 made each of those reads gate= or read it by the shell), and what stays own= is named there with why a tool silent at it publishes nothing by itself: since r8 two -q greps whose answer is their status alone (the chosen-address match, the third until r8, runs no tool: there exit 0 was the PERMISSIVE verdict); the replace-ref listing and the chosen-addresses read, git reads alone whose empty answer is a clone with no replace ref or no user.email, the strict side (since r8b3 an address counts only with the newline git ends it with, so a cut answer chooses fewer addresses); three joins over the scratch listings whose empty answer is the clean verdict of a gate on ANOTHER read or the safe side, so a tool silent there disarms that one gate while the read it gates stands under its status and its other gates; and until r9d the range probe, whose status alone was read, retired with the scan it served (the scanner log's error line, beside the gated count line until r8b3, is gate= since, round 8b3 having found the count line no backstop); since r8b this paragraph is generated from the table's own= prose rows and each own= row states why a cut or empty answer there refuses or publishes nothing; the residual the bound leaves outside the guarantee is two tools silent at once, a listing and its gate (the residual paragraph, with the r7 hook fixer's FLAG 2 on the ruling's closing sentence, that four own= reads still run an awk). A read left outside the helper is declared at its site with the reason (`outside judged_read:`, TWELVE declared reads at the r7b text, counted from the markers: the eight reads made for the report alone, whose failure leaves a refused line with fewer facts and decides nothing (the label's check-attr and the driver key, the blob's size and the threshold key, the parent count's awk over the hook's own pcount file, a read of no object, the rename listing and the previous version's bytes in a parent and in each parent of a merge); one configuration read, the clone's log.showRoot, whose swallowed failure costs an advice line alone and is pinned as such; the ref list on stdin, read by the shell's own builtin since r7; the scanner's own exit, judged from its log; the root-commit probe of the advice arm. Thirteen at the r6b text, where the chosen-addresses read was declared too, through the helper since r7b; this entry had counted twelve there with seven report reads, an undercount of the report's reads by one, since the awk over the hook's own parent-count scratch file is a read command 1 lists no row for, extra9-4: the population below counts it and names it, and its partition sums with twelve at the r7b text, ten of them command 1's rows and two, the ref list's shell read and that awk, command 2's alone). Twelve at the r8b5, r8c and r9b texts too, the same reads at their new lines (the log.showRoot read at the r9b text's 800 since r8, after the ref list at its 782-787; 770 and 752-757 at the r8c text); since r8b every one of them that ran a tool the census reads, ten, had an outside row in tests/pre-push-reads.tsv, keyed to the read by a fixed substring of its own command line, and so did the scanner's argument list (gitleaks_args, the r9b text's 2991; 2911 at r8c), an array literal the census read as a git word, eleven outside rows. TEN since r9d, the log.showRoot read and the root-commit probe retired with the scan they served: the eight report reads, the ref list on stdin (815-820) and the scanner's own exit, since r9d at 3108 in scanner_run and judged with its log against the bytes the feed wrote; eight outside rows, one per report read, the scanner's argument list naming no census tool since r9d (dir . in place of git "$root"), so its row is retired; the ref list's shell read and the scanner's own exit run no such tool and have none.

Command 1 is the round f5 command, run from the repo root; it lists every statement of the hook that names an external read (git, cat, command -v, the gitleaks binary, a helper wrapping one), a judge grep, a file test or the denylist redirect, joined across backslash continuations with comments stripped, and tags each with its syntactic shape (a suppressed, b a substitution with no arm, c the left side of a pipe, d inside a test, s a status read or a refusing arm, R a read, j a judge grep, f a file test, i the input redirect, p report text). Two changes for the r1 text: the two r1 helpers, parent_count and scannable_commits, join the read and pipe-left alternations so their call sites are listed (their inner git reads were caught already), and the arm shape `|| { rc=$?; ... }` joins the status regex. No change for the r2 text: the two new helpers' inner reads are listed on their own statements (refuse_replace_refs' for-each-ref; scannable_commits' two pipelines), and gitleaks_args' argument list is listed once as a false positive (it names git without running it). No change for the r2c text either: refuse_hidden_paths' five reads are plain git statements (ls-tree, diff-tree, check-attr, config, cat-file), each listed on its own line with its status arm, and its loop closers are input redirects, which the command lists; the two predicates' bodies are listed through the file test and the command -v that moved into them; the predicates' call sites and refuse_hidden_paths' own call are not listed, which is right, since none is a read (the helper returns 0 and every read inside it carries its own arm, so its closure is at the helper, not at a caller); mktemp, rm, head and od join the vocabulary cross-check (below). No change for the r2e text either: the showroot_configured read is a plain git statement at the top level, listed as a suppressed substitution with no arm (the one new row); the arm's test reads that variable in place of the config substitution it carried at r2c and is still listed through its rev-list substitution; gitleaks_args' list is listed once as before, `--root` inside its string; the two advice lines, like the r2c advice line, name no read and are not listed. No change for the r3f text either: the check's new git reads are plain git statements, each listed on its own line with its status arm (the tip's `ls-tree -r -z -l` and its read-list grep; a merge's combined patch and its per-parent listing; the empty tree's name; numstat_rows' one diff-tree, listed once at the helper for its three call sites, which read its status like scannable_commits' callers and are not listed themselves, the helper's name being in neither alternation); the label's reads (check-attr per refused path, the driver key, cat-file -s, the config key, the previous version's parent count and bytes) are listed as suppressed reads with no arm and judged safe in the population below; the seven refusing arms of the shape `[ "$rc" -eq 0 ] || { numstat_ok=0; unscanned ... }` and the three `says=` label clauses name git in their text and are listed as R rows the p pattern does not match, set aside as report text below; as_lines' tr and wc and combined_rows' awk run over the hook's own scratch files and are reads of no object (command 2 lists as_lines' statements as truncating filters and substitutions, two at the r3f text, four since r3g and twelve since r5, each with its status arm since r3g, in their row below; tr and wc join the vocabulary cross-check). No change for the r5b text either: the two listing reads whose status is kept apart as listing_rc (819, 876) are listed as bare R rows with no s tag, since the s pattern reads `|| rc=$?` alone, their arms on the next lines (820, 877) as bare R rows the p pattern does not match (it reads `[ "$rc" -eq 0 ] || unscanned` alone), so both are judged from their text; the two counts (847, 1342) are listed as j rows by their grep; the size read (1125), the renames listing (1217) with its closer (1221) and the two previous-version reads (1225, 1231) are plain git statements with their status arms; the short file's cat (1083) a suppressed substitution with no arm, judged safe below; the rev-list --parents test inside the previous-version condition (the r3g text's 1013) is gone, the count read from the caller's pcount file by an awk command 1 does not list; the added lines' capture (1389) is a plain status-read helper call, its judge grep (1391) reading the file; the population awks (853, 1020) and the pcount read (1212) run over the hook's own scratch files and are reads of no object (command 2 lists them as substitutions and truncating filters, in their rows below). No change for the r6b text either, though the tags read differently on it: a judged_read call is listed through the git, grep or helper name after its `--` on the same line (39 of the 53 call lines; the helper's own name is in neither alternation, so the 14 call lines whose command is tr, wc, sed, awk or one of the joins are command 2's alone), tagged a where the call ends in `|| :` (the a pattern reads `|| :` as a suppression; the status is captured in read_rc inside the helper and judged against the call's expected set, so the closure is at the helper and not in the caller's shape) and d where the call is an if head; the three numstat_rows calls (1227, 1228, 1248) are listed for the git their clauses name, R rows the s pattern does not match; the seven report reads command 1 lists (the eighth, the pcount awk, is command 2's alone) and the scanner's exit keep their `|| rc=$?` shapes and are listed as suppressed status reads as before, each marked `outside judged_read` at its site; the two configuration reads and the stdin read are listed as before.
```bash
awk '
{ line = $0
  if (buf != "") line = buf " " line
  if (line ~ /\\$/) { sub(/[ \t]*\\$/, "", line); buf = line; if (!start) start = NR; next }
  n = start ? start : NR; start = 0; buf = ""
  code = line; sub(/^[ \t]*#.*$/, "", code); sub(/[ \t]#[ \t].*$/, "", code)
  reads = "(^|[^A-Za-z0-9_$.-])(git|cat|command -v|\"\\$gl\"|rev_range|added_lines|chosen_emails|is_chosen|stamped_addresses|tag_field|tag_message|parent_count|scannable_commits)([ \t)]|$)"
  judge = "(^|[^A-Za-z0-9_$.-])grep([ \t)]|$)"
  if (code !~ reads && code !~ judge && code !~ /\[ (! )?-[fx] / && code !~ /done < /) next
  shape = ""
  if (code ~ /2>\/dev\/null|>\/dev\/null 2>&1|\|\| *true|\|\| *:( |$)|\|\| *\{ *[a-z_]+=""/) shape = shape "a:suppressed "
  if (code ~ /=\$\(|="\$\(/ && code !~ /\|\|/ && code !~ /^[ \t]*(if|while|elif) /) shape = shape "b:subst-no-arm "
  if (code ~ /(git|cat|added_lines|stamped_addresses|tag_field|tag_message|chosen_emails|parent_count|scannable_commits)[^|]*\| *(grep|awk|while|cut|sed|sort|tr)/) shape = shape "c:pipe-left "
  if (code ~ /^[ \t]*(if|while|elif|case) / || code ~ /\[ "\$\(/ || code ~ /\) *&& *\[/) shape = shape "d:test "
  if (code ~ /\|\| *rc=\$\?|\|\| *\{ *rc=\$\?|\|\| *return \$\?|\|\| *\{ *[a-z_]*=?"?"?;? *(unscanned|tag_unread|type_unread)|\|\| *\{ *$/) shape = shape "s:status-read "
  if (code ~ reads) shape = shape "R "
  if (code ~ judge && code !~ /git grep/) shape = shape "j "
  if (code ~ /\[ (! )?-[fx] /) shape = shape "f:file-test "
  if (code ~ /done < /) shape = shape "i:input-redirect "
  if (code ~ /^[ \t]*(echo|unscanned) / || code ~ /^[ \t]*\[ "\$rc" -eq 0 \] \|\| unscanned/) shape = shape "p:report-text "
  gsub(/^[ \t]+/, "", line)
  printf "%d\t%s\t%s\n", n, shape, line }
' .githooks/pre-push
```

For an older text replace the file argument with `<(git show <sha>:.githooks/pre-push)`. Command 2 is new in r1, for the WIDENED class: a read that succeeds and hands on LESS than its source holds, with no status to read. One row per statement that can lose part of its input or turn "less" into "absent": a `read` loop with what feeds it (L, with file, herestring or pipe: a final line with no newline comes back from `read` with status 1 and ends a plain `while read` before the body runs; a herestring always ends in a newline, a file or a pipe may not), a command substitution (S: strips every trailing newline and every NUL byte), a parameter expansion that cuts the value (P), a truncating filter (T: head, tail, -n, --max-count, an awk exit, sed -n, cut, sort -u, uniq, tr, grep -v, grep .), a read option that reads less than the object by design (N: -I, -l, -M, -c, --not, --remotes, --diff-merges, -U0, --no-show-signature, --format), a test that treats an empty value as absent (E). The hook's only readers of its own captured text are read, `$(...)`, the parameter expansions and the filters, so listing every statement that has one lists every site of the class; the command cannot see whether a given strip LOSES anything, which is what the table judges. Known false positives, left so the listing is reproducible: L on report lines whose text contains "read " and T on the top-level `exit 1`. Command 1 lists a read loop's CLOSER (`done <`) and command 2 its HEAD (`while read`): the head is the row the f5 method could not see, and the denylist reader's head is where the f5 entry was wrong.

```bash
awk '
{ line = $0
  if (buf != "") line = buf " " line
  if (line ~ /\\$/) { sub(/[ \t]*\\$/, "", line); buf = line; if (!start) start = NR; next }
  n = start ? start : NR; start = 0; buf = ""
  code = line; sub(/^[ \t]*#.*$/, "", code); sub(/[ \t]#[ \t].*$/, "", code)
  if (code ~ /^[ \t]*$/) next
  t = ""
  if (code ~ /(^|[ \t;])read[ \t]/ || code ~ /done <|\| *while/) {
      t = t "L:read-loop "
      if (code ~ /done < /) t = t "L:file "
      if (code ~ /done <<< /) t = t "L:herestring "
      if (code ~ /\| *while/) t = t "L:pipe "
  }
  if (code ~ /\$\(/) t = t "S:subst "
  if (code ~ /\$\{[A-Za-z_!#@*0-9]+(%%|%|##|#|:[0-9])/ || code ~ /^[ \t]*case /) t = t "P:param-trim "
  if (code ~ /(^|[ \t|(])(head|tail|uniq|cut|tr|sort -u|sed -n)([ \t]|$)/ || code ~ /--max-count|-n 1( |$)| -1 | exit[ ;}]/ || code ~ /grep -[a-zA-Z]*v|grep \. /) t = t "T:truncate "
  if (code ~ / -I | -l | -M | -c |--not|--remotes|--diff-merges|-U0|--first-parent|--no-show-signature|--format/) t = t "N:narrow "
  if (code ~ /\[ -[nz] |\$\{#|(^|[ \t;&|])(continue|break)([ \t;]|$)|\|\| *return 0/) t = t "E:empty-test "
  if (t == "") next
  gsub(/^[ \t]+/, "", line)
  printf "%d\t%s\t%s\n", n, t, line }
' .githooks/pre-push
```

- 2992-3058 (new in r9d), credential_feed, the pipeline the helper runs whole at 3242 (row 3242-3277): `git diff-tree --stdin -p -U0 -r -M -c --root --always --text --no-color --no-textconv --no-ext-diff --ignore-submodules=none --submodule=short < "$1" | LC_ALL=C tr '\000' '\001' | LC_ALL=C ROMP_LIST_FILE=$1 ROMP_RECORD_FILE=$2 ROMP_PIECE_DIR=$3 ROMP_INDEX_FILE=$4 ROMP_PATH_INDEX_FILE=$5 awk '...'` (2993, a plain R row of command 1 through its git and a T and N row of command 2, its tr and awk on the continued lines): fixed by construction in r9d (the design's 1.2 and 1.3; ruling A.2 (b)). ONE diff-tree reads every commit the ref loop listed, each fed twice, so --always prints the commit's name ahead of its diff and again after it, for the empty diff: the head marker and the tail marker. Every option is explicit (and since round r11c the two inputs that reach diff-tree from outside its options are pinned, below): -c, a merge by the lines in none of its parents, where a line typed into a conflict resolution lands (cases 370 and 371, a merge and an octopus merge named with the file); -M, a rename by its edits; --root, a root commit against the empty tree, plumbing reading no log.showRoot; --text, every blob of a root or one-parent commit as text whatever its bytes, its path's diff attribute or core.bigFileThreshold say, and not a merge's, whose combined diff applies git's binary verdict whatever --text says (round 9's ruling A: executed at eee3938a8, the r9d feed with core.quotePath pinned, a credential in such a merge path published; since round r10a the Binary notice is routed and the path read whole, item 34); --no-color, --no-textconv and --no-ext-diff; --ignore-submodules=none and --submodule=short, a gitlink change its Subproject line. Since round r9e the feed's git runs as `git -c core.quotePath=true diff-tree ...` (this round's other fixer, taking option (a) of the r9d audit's finding 9; case 386 extended with a push under core.quotePath=false that passes as under true, red under the mutant without the -c on both gitleaks versions, and cases 56 and 71 re-aimed to the feed's new command line), so a path's text does not move with the clone's core.quotePath either: under core.quotePath=false a name with a byte over 127 printed raw, and a lockfile name spelled with the Kelvin sign in place of lock's k refused a clean push on the path-scoped run's figure, the safe side. tr maps NUL to 0x01 byte for byte ahead of the awk, so an awk that ends a record at a NUL loses nothing (case 349, a scanner wrapper copying the pieces showing 0x01 where the blob had NUL and every byte after it, red under the mutant whose tr maps nothing; case 341, a NUL line then a credential; both red at cad898dd2 by publication, where gitleaks' git mode read no line of such a file). The awk reads the list file diff-tree reads through the environment, never a -v operand; checks each commit's head marker in the order fed (3029-3033: a missing, foreign or out-of-order line records unmarked <n>, and the awk keeps draining, so diff-tree is never cut off mid-write) and its tail marker (3034; without it the END records untailed <n>, 3054); compares names as strings (`$0 "" == sha`, since gawk and mawk compare two all-digit strings as numbers); writes each file's added lines, commit by commit, to pieces, each opened by a ~ line, so an archive or document signature at a file's byte 0 moves off it (case 372, a zip signature first, passes clean; case 373, a PDF signature first with a credential below, is refused naming it; each red under the mutant without the ~ line), each at most 98,304 bytes, under gitleaks' 100,000-byte read of a file, a continuation repeating the last 16,384 bytes and a line longer than 81,916 bytes cut into overlapping windows (case 374, the chunk witness, a credential at byte 124,976 of a 3,000,001-byte line, red by publication under the mutant whose cap passes the line; case 375, the piece-boundary witness, a PGP private key block across the cap, red by publication under the mutant with no overlap); writes the index (each piece's number, commit and file, and a closing field) and the path-scoped index (a file whose name ends in one of the five path-scoped rules' suffixes, less the names gitleaks' global path allowlist skips under that name), stripping git's trailing tab after a path holding a space (the r9d hook fixer's FLAG 2, so a spaced .yaml, .tf, .php, .p12 or nuget.config name reaches the additive run; the r9d audit: a Secret in `my secret.yaml` refused naming that file); writes a commit two refs share once (the seen set; case 378, the byte figure one ref's, which no mutant turns red on this git, whose diff-tree --stdin prints a repeated commit's diff once: the r9d hook fixer's FLAG 4); and records one line (unmarked <n>, untailed <n>, short <done>, nolist, or four counts: commits read whole, pieces, bytes written, bytes of the path-scoped pieces). Its status, under pipefail, is diff-tree's, tr's or the awk's, a kill included, judged at the call. The table's figures, by derivation, at the texts the result paragraph after the command names (R=HEAD, and the texts it compares). Since r8b the population is judged by the table's rule and no longer listed by tag: tests/pre-push-reads.tsv holds one row per tool read of the hook, a judged_read tag's row keyed to the tag and an outside row keyed to its read by a fixed substring of the read's command line, each with the fact that shows a whole answer and, since r8b3, after "Its end:" what bounds the answer's END, or the reason an empty or cut-short exit-0 answer there refuses or publishes nothing; the case that drives the read with its tool silent and the case that drives it cut short (or, on a gate= or own= row alone, "none:" and the reason no cut applies), each through a real push; and its class and its end kind. The table case (tests/pre-push-hook.bats 227) is red on a read with no row, a row with no read, a row whose case matches no @test line or pushes through no hook, a short case that asserts no cut, a fact with no end or an end of no known kind, since round r8c an outside row whose short column is none: with a reason (an outside row names its short case, because a cut of a read made for the report can change what the report says), since round r9b a case or a short case whose title does not carry `: <read>:`, the case of another read (the trailing colon refusing a read whose name is a prefix of a neighbour's), and a header block that differs from the output of tests/pre-push-reads-header.sh by a byte, read since round r9b from the first BEGIN marker alone, so a doubled or inserted marker line is no longer dropped before the comparison. The command below, run from the repo root, derives the figures from the committed files (R the text read, B the r7b text for the change of tags):

```bash
R=HEAD B=1185a7e43
T=$(git show "$R:tests/pre-push-reads.tsv"); H=$(git show "$R:.githooks/pre-push")
rows() { awk -F'\t' '$1 ~ /^(gate|own|outside)$/' <<< "$T"; }
count() { sort | uniq -c | sort -k1,1nr -k2 | awk '{ printf "%s %s; ", $2, $1 }'; echo; }
echo "rows by kind: $(awk -F'\t' '!/^#/ && NF { print $1 }' <<< "$T" | count)"
echo "prose by paragraph: $(awk -F'\t' '$1 == "prose" { print $2 }' <<< "$T" | count)"
echo "classes: $(rows | cut -f6 | count)"
echo "end kinds: $(rows | cut -f8 | count)"
echo "facts stating an end: $(rows | cut -f3 | grep -c 'Its end: ')"
echo "silent cases: $(rows | cut -f4 | sort -u | wc -l) distinct for $(rows | wc -l) rows"
echo "short cases: $(rows | cut -f7 | grep -v '^none: ' | sort -u | wc -l) distinct; none: $(rows | awk -F'\t' '$7 ~ /^none: / { print $1 }' | count)"
for col in 4 7; do rows | cut -f"$col" | grep -v '^none: ' | while IFS= read -r ref; do
  n=$(git show "$R:tests/pre-push-${ref%%:*}.bats" | grep -F -- "${ref#*:}" | grep -c '^@test ')
  [ "$n" -eq 1 ] || echo "column $col: $ref matches $n @test lines"; done; done
echo "facts recording a round 8b3 publication: $(rows | cut -f3 | grep -c 'round 8b3: .*publish')"
echo "tags: gate= $(grep -c 'judged_read gate="' <<< "$H") call sites, $(grep -oE 'judged_read gate="[^"]*"' <<< "$H" | sort -u | wc -l) names; own= $(grep -c 'judged_read own="' <<< "$H"), $(grep -oE 'judged_read own="[^"]*"' <<< "$H" | sort -u | wc -l)"
diff <(git show "$B:.githooks/pre-push" | grep -oE 'judged_read (gate|own)="[^"]*"' | sort -u) <(grep -oE 'judged_read (gate|own)="[^"]*"' <<< "$H" | sort -u) | sed -n 's/^\([<>]\) judged_read /  \1 /p'
echo "hook $(wc -l <<< "$H") lines, block $(awk '/^# BEGIN generated block/ { p = 1; next } /^# END generated block/ { p = 0 } p' <<< "$H" | wc -l); cases: $(for f in pre-push-hook pre-push-identity pre-push-message install-sh; do printf '%s ' "$(git show "$R:tests/$f.bats" | grep -c '^@test ')"; done)"
```
