33. The read inside gitleaks, taken into the hook, r9d (cited by name since round r10c, ruling I, where these were the r9d text's line numbers: list_commits; credential_feed; gitleaks_args; scanner_run; scan_credentials; unscanned_n, the counter and its reads in list_commits; the header's credential paragraph and its scratch paragraph; the table's CREDENTIAL FEED and BYTE COUNT line rows, the ERR line row's fact, seven rows retired, and the header regenerated; tests/pre-push-hook.bats cases 369 to 388 new in a round 9d section and 22 slots re-aimed in place; tests/install-sh.bats, tests/gitleaks-config.bats and tests/gitleaks-require.bats; CLAUDE.md's credentials section): round 8's refuters found, on the r8c text and by real pushes, the credential scan's coverage check whole at the granularity of commits and no finer (extra5-1, ruling A.1): gitleaks ran git log -p itself and counted a commit once one of its files had a hunk, so a stream its git cut inside a commit, the later files' diffs dropped with nothing on stderr, passed the ERR arm and the count arm whatever git's exit status, and a credential in a dropped file published, through a faulty git on PATH and through a real git killed partway, under gitleaks 8.30.1 and 8.28.0. Ruling A.2 put three options to the repository owner (a byte or hunk figure the hook derives itself; the hook's own added lines handed to the scanner; the cut accepted as a stated residual), and the owner took option (b) (romp-manager for the owner, 2026-09-24), with flags 87, 88 and 89 accepted; romp-manager's three rulings on the design set its shape: the five path-scoped rules kept by an additive run whose byte figure is checked like the main run's; the feed read as the added lines of a combined and rename-aware diff (-c -M), so a merge that brings in content a remote already holds and a pure rename of a published file no longer refuse; and directory mode over pieces, at the cost that gitleaks' built-in lockfile and vendored-tree path exemptions no longer reach the hook's scan, the fork's allowlist covering any false alarm. BUILT in r9d (design-b-final.md): the credential scan lists each ref's commits through list_commits, the identifier scan's listing and its gates moved into a function both scans share, and reads every line they add in ONE git diff-tree --stdin over the push (credential_feed: each commit fed twice for its head and tail markers; -c, -M, --root, --text and every other option explicit; NUL mapped to 0x01 ahead of the awk; and since round r9e the feed's git run as git -c core.quotePath=true, so path text does not move with the clone's configuration; case 386 extended with a push under core.quotePath=false that passes as under true, red under the mutant without the -c on both gitleaks versions, and cases 56 and 71 re-aimed to the feed's new command line); the awk writes the added lines to pieces of at most 98,304 bytes, each opened by a ~ line, a continuation repeating the last 16,384 bytes, and records the commits read whole, the pieces and the bytes; gitleaks scans the pieces in directory mode from inside their directory and runs no git (scanner_run), judged by its exit, any line at its ERR level and its one byte figure against the bytes written, each finding named with its commit and file from the piece's name; and the additive run scans copies of the files the five path-scoped rules name, made by the shell (under their basenames until round 10b, under a fixed stem since, item 34), with those rules alone. RETIRED with the scan they served, each row gone and each case slot re-aimed in place with the read it held named in its title (R5): scannable_commits and the COUNT of commits with content to scan, the RANGE probe, the REPOSITORY ROOT, the COLOUR STRIP, the COMMIT COUNT line, the log.showRoot read and its advice arm with the root-commit probe, and gitleaks_args' git-mode list. THE COSTS AND CHANGES, stated at the header and pinned: directory mode skips gitleaks' built-in path exemptions (lockfiles, node_modules and vendored trees, images and the rest of its global allowlist), so a credential-shaped string in such a file is refused and a false alarm is excused in .gitleaks.toml by exact value (case 387, a token in package-lock.json refused); the five path-scoped rules are kept by the additive run with its figure checked (cases 380 to 386 and 388); a merge that brings in content a remote already holds and a pure rename of a published file no longer refuse (cases 376 and 377, the witnesses of romp-manager's second ruling, each red at cad898dd2 by refusal); a match with more than 16,384 of its bytes before a piece boundary is whole in neither piece (so stated since round r10b, ruling C: until then this item said a match longer than 16,384 bytes, false for a match with at most 16,384 of its bytes before the boundary, which lies whole in the next piece), and a piece whose bytes carry a binary type's signature at its fixed offset is skipped by gitleaks and refused by the byte figure, loudly (both disclosed at the header); and a bare repository scans under the default rules, where it was refused before. Decided by execution at both texts (the r9d hook fixer, the r9d gltests fixer and the r9d audit, real pushes under gitleaks 8.30.1 and 8.28.0): the refuters' within-commit cut published at cad898dd2 in all three exit modes (exit 0, exit 1, a SIGKILL) and is refused at the r9d text naming the feed (cases 332, 269 and 333); of the 45 cases titled round 9d, 44 are red at cad898dd2 on both scanners, the 45th a kept control (352), and of sixteen named mutants each turns its cases red but the seen set's (the r9d hook fixer's FLAG 4); the four hook bats files and the two gitleaks files 536 of 536 on each scanner (388, 42, 30 and 59; 14 and 3); the contract shapes 34 of 34 identical for the identifier scan; the class probe 22 of 22, its noise and short modes, keyed on gitleaks' own git log, driving no read since; the matrices 59 of 59 and 16 of 16; the tip check over 3,193 regular files, 41 skipped, none of them text; the whole history pushed as a new ref with the scan armed, 178,327,961 bytes in the main run and 66,453 in the additive run, no leaks, on both scanners; and the cost per push about unchanged (the r9d audit's bench, the median of three real pushes: 494, 509 and 801 ms at the r9b text and 472, 523 and 811 ms at the r9d text, at 1, 20 and 300 commits).

THE COLOUR ROAD, the ninth, of the widened class, on two statements of the r1 text (523 scannable_commits and 598 the gitleaks invocation; on the r2 text the derivation at 569-583 and the invocation at 674, on the r2c text 749-763 and 853, on the r2e text 778-792 and 885), FIXED in r1 by the round's audit and, as the first found instance of the class the next paragraph states, closed structurally in r2; no OPEN row remains on the committed text. The fault: a stream that holds less than the objects, read alike by both readers, so the "independent" count agrees with the emptied scan. With `color.ui=always` or `color.diff=always` in any config scope git reads (the clone's, or a user's global gitconfig, which reaches gitleaks' git the same way), git colours `log -p` written to a pipe. gitleaks 8.30.1 and 8.28.0 find no file header in the SGR-prefixed stream, report `0 commits scanned` and `no leaks found`, write no ERR line (git wrote nothing to stderr) and exit 0; the fixer's scannable_commits read the same coloured stream and its `^diff --git ` and `^@@` patterns missed the same lines, so expected 0 equalled reported 0, both conditions passed, and the hook exited 0. By execution: the control refuses as a finding on every text; with either key set, a REAL push through the installed hook, the denylist armed, published a branch carrying two live credentials assembled at run time with nothing printed but gitleaks' two INF lines, at the f5 text and at the fixer's r1 text alike. gitleaks scanning nothing under colour is pre-existing (every text before r1 passes there too); what r1's first text added was a count condition derived from the same `git log -p` stream, which therefore shared every upstream transform with the scan it checked: a count derived from the same stream is independent of the scanner's PARSING, not of the stream's TRANSFORMS. The fix, the smallest edit on both statements: `git log --no-color -p -U0 ...` in scannable_commits, so the derivation reads a stream it has cleaned itself, and `--log-opts="$range --diff-merges=first-parent --no-color"` on the gitleaks invocation, so the scanner's git is given the same (an option on the log outranks color.diff=always in every scope). The first alone was tried and is not enough: the secrets push is refused `covered 0 of the 2` (fail-closed) but a CLEAN push under colour is refused too (`covered 0 of the 1`: gitleaks still scans nothing), so both reads get the option. The `--log-opts` form rather than a `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` prefix on the invocation (also verified to work): tests/git-hermetic.bash exports `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT=5` with five pairs for every bats run, and a prefix of 2 would override them for gitleaks' git. Comments at the three sites say why: one sentence in the hook's header paragraph, a paragraph at the end of scannable_commits' comment, two lines in the invocation's comment. Two bats cases at the end of the gitleaks section of tests/pre-push-hook.bats: a credential under `color.ui=always` is refused as a FINDING (the coloured stream shown by execution first, then the hook: status 1, `2 commits scanned`, the github-pat rule named, `gitleaks found a credential`, and not the incomplete-scan line), and a clean push under `color.diff=always` passes with `1 commits scanned` and no hook line, so the counts agree for the right reason. Fails-before: against the f5 text the file runs 1..50 with 41 ok and 9 not ok (the fixer's seven, plus 49 at `[ "$status" -eq 1 ]`, the fail-open, and 50 at `1 commits scanned`, since gitleaks reported 0 there); against the fixer's r1 text 48 ok and 2 not ok (49 and 50 at the same assertions). So case 50 is not a bare pass control: it pins that gitleaks reads the commit under colour, the scanner-side half of the repair. The real-push probe with the committed hook, gitleaks 8.30.1 and 8.28.0: the control refused as a finding; the noise mode refused on both clauses; the short mode by the count alone (`1 of the 2`); the fail mode on both; the rename warning refused as a finding on the secrets branch and passing on the clean one; a clean push exit 0 with 0 hook lines; under `color.ui=always` and under `color.diff=always` the secrets branch refused as a FINDING (`2 commits scanned`, `leaks found: 2`) and the clean push passing (`1 commits scanned`); the remote holding main alone after every run. The identifier arm is unaffected (its tip grep has `--no-color` since r1, diff-tree since f5, and ls-tree, cat-file, rev-list, config and `log --format` print no colour, as check-attr -z and `cat-file blob` since r2c, and the read-list grep with `--no-color`, the numstats with -z and a merge's combined patch with `--no-color`, the same read as added_lines, since r3 to r3d; all five hit kinds named under `color.ui=always`). Beside it, the widened method surfaced boundaries that need a deliberate configuration, recorded at r1 for a follow-up and not counted as roads; review round r2 found by execution that they were instances of the class and closed all but half of one: B1 a content-hiding textconv that succeeds silently (at r1 gitleaks scanned the converted, empty content and the derivation counted the same commit, and a REAL push published a credential at the r1 text; CLOSED in r2 by the object derivation, with `--no-textconv` in gitleaks_args so a clean push agrees and the dirty push is refused as a finding); B2 a `-diff` or `binary` attribute on a text file, in two halves: the credential half (gitleaks' `log -p` printed no hunk for the file, and a real push published at the r1 text) CLOSED in r2 by `--text`, under which gitleaks scans what git would call binary and the derivation counts every changed non-empty blob, binary or not; the identifier half OPEN on the r2 text as a documented boundary: `git grep -I` takes the attribute's binary as binary and `diff-tree -p` prints no added line for such a file, so a banned string in a text file under a -diff attribute published through the identifier arm at the r2 text (shown by the round's auditor with a real push, the denylist armed and gitleaks off), the documented binary boundary widened from content to attribute; not closed by a `--text` on the identifier arm, which would change a documented contract (a binary file's string is a pass), and ruled IN-FOLD and CLOSED in r2c by REFUSAL rather than by scanning, the shape refuse_replace_refs set, refuse_hidden_paths at r2c reading the diff attribute of every blob the two content reads would read; that closure was keyed on the attribute's VALUE, and round r3 found it FALSE by execution (a text blob over core.bigFileThreshold in a middle commit and one under a driver named a reserved word each published through a real push at 89589e7d3, the size key being a way git calls a blob binary that no attribute names), so since r3 the half is closed by git's OWN VERDICT on each read: the tip's grep asked what it read, and the per-commit diff's numstat over the pair it reads, or a merge's own combined patch, asked what it printed no hunk for, and a blob so skipped whose bytes are text is refused rather than read as empty, whatever rule made git call it binary, so the contract stands (a blob binary by its bytes passes under any attribute or key, contract shape c6) and hidden text is never read as empty; per-commit ONLY, since the size key does not reach the grep and a tip-side size rule would be a false refusal, and an explicit `diff` attribute outranks the key in every read and prints the hunk, so it is a hit and never a refusal (the paragraph after the class paragraph); B3 a replace ref (every read of both arms followed the replacement while `git push` transferred the ORIGINAL object: a real push published the banned string and the credential at the pushed sha; a third shape beside "fails" and "less": a read that returns OTHER than the pushed object) CLOSED in r2 by `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1` exported for every git the hook runs and a refusal before either scan under any replace ref (refuse_replace_refs); B4 the over-refusal under `format.pretty` set to a non-default format (oneline, raw, email among them: gitleaks parses the default header, attributes no commit, and a CLEAN push was refused `covered 0 of the 1`) CLOSED in r2 by `--pretty=medium` in gitleaks_args, with `--date=default` beside it for the same over-refusal under `log.date` set to relative or human, found in r2; B5 `log.showSignature=true` with a signed commit git cannot verify, labelled at r1 an over-refusal by assessment and shown by execution in r2 to be worse (on the refuters' runner the f5 text PUBLISHED a credential under it with `0 commits scanned` and the r1 text refused clean pushes; on the fold's own box gpg's report went into the log's stdout and gitleaks parsed past it) CLOSED in r2 by `--no-show-signature` in gitleaks_args, which the hook's two other log reads already carried. No boundary of the five stays open on the r2c text, nor on the r2e text, where the r2c flags were taken as ruled (the paragraph after the class paragraph). On the r2e text the identifier half was open again by the enumeration's gap, found and closed by derivation in round r3; rounds r3c to r3f closed the roads and the false refusals the derivation's first scope left (a symlink's target over the size key; a merge's symlink under an attribute; an empty file a merge adds; a merge's third-path move), so no boundary of the five stays open on the r3f text.

THE CLASS BEHIND THE COLOUR ROAD, transforms of a stream both readers shared, found by review round r2 and closed structurally in the same round (2026-09-21). The mechanism below held until round r9d, which retired the count and gitleaks' own git read with it (item 33): since r9d the credential feed is plumbing given every option explicitly and gitleaks reads only the bytes the hook wrote, so no transform of a stream reaches either. The r1 fix gave the hook two coverage conditions so one failing could not hide the other, and its own audit found they were not independent under colour and patched that with `--no-color`. Round r2 found five more transforms of the same `git log -p` stream on the committed r1 text, each by execution: a `.gitattributes` `diff=<driver>` entry with a hiding `diff.<driver>.textconv`, and a `-diff` attribute on the file carrying the credential, each rewrote or emptied the patch for gitleaks AND for the hook's count, so both conditions passed and a REAL push through the installed hook published a live credential assembled at run time with zero hook lines; `format.pretty` set to a non-default format and `log.date` set to relative each broke gitleaks' header parse while its content coverage stayed whole, so a CLEAN push was refused `covered 0 of the 1` with advice pointing at disabling the scan; and `log.showSignature=true` with an unverifiable signature wrote gpg's report into the stream (the refuters' runner: the f5 text published, the r1 text refused clean pushes). Six transforms in two rounds, three of which published a credential while the hook reported clean and three of which refused clean pushes, from one mechanism: a count derived from the stream it checks is only as independent as the stream is clean, and patching transforms one at a time keeps finding roads (romp-manager's ruling for the round, section A: a wrong mechanism, not a missing case). The fix takes the count off the stream: scannable_commits reads the OBJECTS, `git rev-list --parents` over the range (every commit gitleaks' log walks, each with its first parent), one `git diff-tree --stdin -r --raw --no-renames --root` over those pairs (each commit's changed paths against its first parent, a root against the empty tree, with their blob shas, applying no attribute, driver or colour) and one `git cat-file --batch-check` over the added and type-changed blobs; a commit has content when one entry that is not a deletion gives the patch a hunk: a gitlink whose sha changed (a `Subproject commit` line either way), a modified blob whose sha changed (a mode-only change keeps the sha and prints no hunk; a file emptied prints one), or a new or type-changed path whose blob is not empty (an empty new file or link prints no hunk); a blob cat-file cannot find exits 3 with a fatal line and refuses, and any of the three reads failing refuses at the caller (`a git read exited N`), never counting 0. The plain-stream options are hardening, not the fix: gitleaks_args builds gitleaks' argument list in ONE place, which the hook's invocation and tests/pre-push-hook.bats's scan_direct (which evals the function out of the hook file) both call, so the two match by construction; its `--log-opts` value carries the range, `--diff-merges=first-parent` and nine options at r2, ten since r2h, each outranking the config key it neutralises in every scope: `--no-color`, `--no-textconv`, `--text` (a -diff attribute or core.bigFileThreshold makes git call a file binary and print no hunk for it; under the option gitleaks scans what git would call binary, and the derivation counts every changed non-empty blob, binary or not), `--no-renames` (a pure rename is a deletion and an addition for both reads), `--submodule=short` and `--ignore-submodules=none` (diff.submodule and diff.ignoreSubmodules otherwise drop a gitlink hunk from gitleaks' stream and refuse a clean push), `--no-show-signature`, `--pretty=medium` and `--date=default`, and since r2h `--root` (log.showRoot, below); three of the nine r2 options (`--no-renames` and the two submodule options) are the round's fixer's additions beyond the ruling's list, each a false-refusal remedy recorded in the round's log with its alternative. Verified by execution: the hook's own scannable_commits and gitleaks_args, read out of the hook file, against gitleaks 8.30.1 and 8.28.0 over 40 commit shapes (round r1's 22, with the binary-only and rename-only commits now counted by all three; eleven typechange and gitlink shapes; files with and without a trailing newline; a replace ref with and without the export) and over the fork's own HEAD~300..HEAD range at 3a05b63bb, the round 2 commit's parent and the round 2 fixer's staged-tree base (300 first-parent steps; 4513 commits by rev-list; 4502 with content on all three counts, the derivation and both versions; the figures derived here once and referred to from the count read's row above and from the body: re-derived in round r6c from the committed hook's scannable_commits over 3a05b63bb~300..3a05b63bb, `git rev-list --count` 4513, `--first-parent` 300, the derivation 4502 in 0.8 s, and gitleaks 8.30.1 and 8.28.0 each reporting `4502 commits scanned` and `no leaks found` under gitleaks_args' log options, in 59 s each), 0 mismatches; 36 diff- and log-affecting config keys swept, every one passing through under the final option set (two, diff.submodule and diff.ignoreSubmodules, needed the two submodule options); the derivation takes 0.8 s over that range where the r1 patch read took 44 s, and gitleaks itself 55 s under `--text --no-renames` where it took 45 s, a push faster overall. `log.showRoot` set to false, which hides a root commit's diff from gitleaks' log while the derivation counts the root's entries, was named at r2 and r2c as a fail-closed over-refusal rather than a road (a push carrying a root commit refused `covered N-1 of the N commits with content to scan`, the r1 text having published a credential in a root commit under the key; since r2c one line after the coverage line naming the key and the remedy) on the premise that no option turns the root's diff back on. The premise was false: round r2e found by execution that `--root` outranks the key (`git log -p --root` under the key set false prints the root's hunk, two hunks with the option and one without, under git 2.43.0 and both gitleaks versions), so romp-manager's ruling of 2026-09-21 15:52Z, which on that premise had reversed the refusal to an environment override (a GIT_CONFIG_COUNT pair setting the key true for the hook's child gits, staged in r2e with a refusal arm for a scanner whose git does not inherit it), was replaced in round r2h by the fold owner with the option itself (rulings-r2h.md, flagged for romp-manager's confirmation in round 3; the pair is recoverable from the r2e fixer log). NEUTRALISED: `--root` is the tenth option in gitleaks_args' `--log-opts`, the shape `--pretty` and `--date` take, and it outranks the key in every scope and travels on the scanner's command line, so a root commit's diff is scanned (its credential found as a finding), a clean root push passes with the scanner's count the hook's own, and a scanner git that does not inherit the hook's environment still reads it. REFUSES: a scanner whose git does not honour the option, or any other count that comes up short, is refused on the count as before, and when the short count meets the key false in the clone's configuration (showroot_configured, the file value read once at the top of the hook) and a root commit in the range, the refusal also names the key, says the scanner's git did not honour the `--root` option the hook passes on its log, and gives the remedy (set the key to true or unset it and push again, or `ROMP_NO_GITLEAKS=1` for that one push). The ruling's reason (romp-manager's, restated by the fold owner for the option): fail-closed is the fallback for what the hook cannot see, not the answer when it can neutralise the cause; a refusal a contributor must fix by editing their own git config, for something the hook could have ignored, is a false refusal; the key belongs in the hardening list beside format.pretty and log.date; and the option is the narrowest mechanism, one word on one command line, where the pair injected configuration into every git the hook runs. Beyond the replacement switch the hook exports nothing for the gits it runs (a caller's GIT_CONFIG pairs pass through untouched, a negative pin). The two halves of the hook differ in KIND, stated at its header: since r9d the credential feed is plumbing given each option explicitly, since r11c with the inputs that reach it from outside its options pinned (GIT_DIFF_OPTS unset, diff.suppressBlankEmpty=false; ruling C, extra5-1), and gitleaks reads only the bytes the hook wrote (from r2 to r9b the credential half's own count was derived from the objects and no configuration touched it, while the scanner inside it read a log stream); the identifier half reads a log and diff stream, so it is config-dependent by construction. What can hide a blob from its two content reads is closed since r3 by a DERIVED PROPERTY rather than key by key: git's own verdict on each read against the blob's bytes (refuse_hidden_paths: git produced no hunk for a blob that is text), which covers the diff attribute, core.bigFileThreshold, a driver under any name and a rule a later git adds with one mechanism and no enumeration to keep current, in a one-parent commit's diff and in a merge's combined patch alike. That sentence is what keeps the next key from reopening this entry, and the enumeration that failed is why it is the sentence: at r2c the half was hardened by an enumeration of attribute values (round r2c's attribute road closed by `unset`, a binary-true driver and the two reserved words that do not hide), and round r3 found the size key and check-attr's reserved words outside it by real pushes, the same class round r2 had ruled on for the credential half (a guard keyed on a name, a spelling or a value rather than on the property), so the entry keys on the effect and not on the cause. The scanner's log was hardened key by key as keys were found until r9d, when gitleaks stopped reading a log (log.showRoot, by `--root` on the scanner's log, in r2e and r2h), and so are the identifier half's other reads (colour on its grep, the signature report on its log, each by the option that outranks the key); reading the identifier half's input from the objects is the long fix, named and not taken in this fold. Beside the class, the premise the whole hook rests on is stated at the hook's header and enforced: both scans read the objects at the pushed shas on the premise that what they read is what the push transfers; a replace ref breaks it (every read followed the substitute while the push transferred the original, and a real push published a banned string and a credential at the pushed sha with both scans reading clean), so `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1` is exported beside `set -euo pipefail` for every git the hook runs (gitleaks' own included until r9d; it runs none since), and refuse_replace_refs, run before either scan, refuses the push under any `refs/replace/` ref (or under the namespace `GIT_REPLACE_REF_BASE` names), naming each ref and the premise: a scan under replacement cannot vouch for the bytes pushed. At the r2 text the refusal was unconditional (a clone with no denylist and no gitleaks was refused too when it carried one), the ruling's text taken and the alternative (refuse only when a scan is armed) recorded in the round's log; round r2c ruled for the alternative and NARROWED the gate: refuse_replace_refs lists the replace refs only when identifier_scan_armed (the denylist file exists, at ROMP_PRIVATE_STRINGS or the XDG default, through private_strings_file) or credential_scan_armed (ROMP_NO_GITLEAKS unset and a gitleaks binary resolving through ROMP_GITLEAKS or PATH and executable, through gitleaks_binary) holds, the two predicates scan_identifiers' and scan_credentials' own early returns read, so the gate and the scans agree by construction, and the predicates decide only whether a scan runs (an empty denylist arms a scan that greps for nothing; a skipped or absent scanner keeps its silence or its notice). The reason, the ruling's: the refusal prevents a false CLEAN REPORT, and where no scan runs there is no report and nothing false to prevent, so a clone that never asked for either scan stays unaffected, as CLAUDE.md promises (the identifier scan a no-op on an absent denylist; gitleaks absent a loud notice and no scan). By execution through real pushes at the r2c text: a replace ref with neither scan armed passes with no replace line (the not-installed notice printing where the binary is absent and the skip not asked), and each scan armed alone is refused on the premise; at the r2 text the first two were refused. CLAUDE.md's gitleaks bullet states the refusal class for a contributor refused by it: a gitleaks that ran but cannot show what it scanned (an error line in its own log, a count that is not the hook's own, no count at all) refuses the push, and since r2h says the hook passes `--root` on the scanner's log so a root commit's diff is scanned whatever `log.showRoot` says, and that a scanner whose git does not honour it refuses on the count and says so, naming the key and the remedy. Since r9d the bullet says that the hook reads for itself the changes a push would publish, a merge by its combined diff, and hands those bytes to gitleaks (exactly those bytes until round 11d, which dropped the word once the probe run's own texts made it false), which runs no git, and names the refusal class as an error line in gitleaks' log, a scanned-byte figure that is not the count of bytes the hook handed it, or no figure at all; its log.showRoot sentence is gone. In tests/pre-push-hook.bats (388 cases since r9d, 368 at r9b, 367 at r9a, 354 at r8b5, 353 at r8b4, 352 at r8b3, 291 at r8b2, 227 at r8b, 212 at r8, 207 at r7b, 202 at r7, 193 at r6b, 185 at r6, 173 at r5b, 167 at r5, 150 at r3g, 146 at r3f, 139 at r3e, 134 at r3d, 123 at r3c, 111 at r3 after its audit's one repair, 85 since r2e and r2h, 80 at r2c, 65 at r2, 50 at r1): the two refusal arms round r1 left untested are pinned (case 51, a stub scanner exiting 0 with no count line: `reported no commit count`; case 52, the hook's own count read refused alone by a `fail_diff_tree_stdin` wrapper over `git_refusing`: `could not be counted for the credential scan (a git read exited 128)`), each red when its own arm is deleted from the hook and green when the other's is; the shapes control (case 45) is re-cut for the resolved invocation (seven commits, three counted: a binary add and a pure rename count as their bytes say; an empty commit, a deletion, a mode-only change and an empty new file for nothing); scan_direct reads gitleaks_args out of the hook (a hook that renames the function fails the helper loudly rather than drifting from it); and thirteen transform cases (53 to 65, with the helpers hiding_textconv, signed_commit and substitute_for): one dirty push per transform refused as a FINDING (the textconv, the -diff attribute, format.pretty=oneline, log.date=relative, the signed commit under log.showSignature=true) and one clean push per setting passing with the scanner's own count and no hook line, the replace ref refused on the premise line with the finding beside it (dirty) and on the premise alone (clean), and log.showRoot=false refused on the count alone (65 at r2 and r2c; re-aimed in r2e and r2h to the credential FOUND, below). Fails-before, the new bats file against the r1 text (3a05b63bb), recorded per case and not as "each red before": of the 18 cases added or changed in r2, twelve are red at the r1 text by their property (45, 52, 53, 55, 56, 57, 58, 59, 60, 63, 64, 65: the textconv, -diff, replace-ref and showRoot dirty pushes published there, the format.pretty and log.date clean pushes were refused there, and the -diff clean control read `1 commits scanned` with the file skipped as binary), two red by construction only (46 and 47, whose scan_direct reads gitleaks_args out of a hook that has none), and four green at the r1 text (51, whose arm exists there and is pinned by the mutation; 54, the clean textconv control; 61 and 62, the signed commit, green on the fold's box, whose gpg writes its report where gitleaks parses past it, so those two pin the scanner-side option by reading the raw log for gpg). Round r2c added or changed 18 more, counted apart from r2's 18: fifteen new (66 to 80) and three changed (55 and 56, the credential half's two -diff cases, run with the denylist absent, since the armed identifier scan now refuses their pushes on the attribute before the property they pin is reached, the property unchanged and nothing retired; 65, which asserts the showRoot line): 66 the no-key control; 67 to 70 the gate under its four arming states (neither scan; the credential scan not skipped but no gitleaks on PATH, through path_without_gitleaks; the denylist alone; gitleaks alone); 71 to 78 the attribute road in eight forms, the first two through REAL pushes with the hook installed and the remote's state asserted (push_main_through_hook, remote_holds_main, attributes); 79 and 80 the check's own reads against their refusing inputs. Against the r2 text (b42231339), per case: ten red by their property (65, the key line absent; 67 and 68, refused with neither scan armed; 71, 72, 74, 76 and 77, the -diff text file published or passed through the identifier arm, in the tree, under a binary driver, in .git/info/attributes and in a middle commit; 79 and 80, reads the r2 text did not make) and eight green (55 and 56, the re-aimed pair; 66, no key line under either text; 69 and 70, refused under the unconditional gate too; 73, the binary blob's pass; 75, the textconv-only driver's hit; 78, the no-denylist no-op). Rounds r2e and r2h, one commit, added or changed six more, counted apart (r2e's own count at its index text, 84 cases: four new, 66, 67, 68 and 81, and 65 re-aimed to the credential FOUND under the pair, red before at 65 66 67 68 81; r2h then added one and re-aimed four from the pair to the option): five new (66 a clean root under log.showRoot=false passes; 67 the negative pin, the hook exports no GIT_CONFIG pair of its own, the caller's one pair seen alone in the scanner's git's command scope through gitleaks_git_recording_pairs; 68 the environment-stripping scanner wrapper, gitleaks_without_config_pairs, still scans the root, the option travelling on the command line; 69 the option-stripping wrapper, gitleaks_without_root_option, refused on the count with the arm line in full and the recorded option list the hook's own less that one word; 82 a pure rename of a hidden text file the remote already holds, through a real fixture push, refused with the rename clause and the explicit-diff remedy) and one re-aimed (65, the root commit's credential FOUND as a finding, its road shown first by scan_direct_without_root, the hook's own list less the option, with a pin that the list carries it); r2c's 66 to 80 are 70 to 85, 82 between them. Against the r2c text (e744c28d4), per case: 65, 66, 67, 68, 69 and 82 red by their property (65 and 66 first at scan_direct_without_root's pin, 65 also at the finding and the scanner's own count; 67 and 68 at the pass, refused on the count there; 69 at the re-aimed line; 82 at the explicit-diff remedy), 70 green under both texts; against the r2e index text (the pair), 65 to 69 red (67 at the command-scope equality, r2e's hook having appended its pair; 68 at the pass, refused there; 69 at the coverage line, the pair having found the credential where the option's absence should have refused), 70 and 82 green. THE IDENTIFIER SCAN'S OWN INSTANCE OF THE CLASS, the identifier half of boundary B2, found open by the r2 audit by execution, closed in round r2c by refusal rather than by scanning, found OPEN AGAIN by review round r3 (2026-09-21) by execution, the r2c closure having been keyed on an enumeration, and closed since r3 by a DERIVED PROPERTY, git's own verdict on each read, its scope made equal to the property's by rounds r3c to r3f (2026-09-22). Both content reads of the identifier arm read what git calls TEXT: the tip's `git grep -I` skips a blob git calls binary and the per-commit `diff-tree -p` prints no line for one. At r2c the entry, the hook's header and CLAUDE.md enumerated how git calls a blob binary (its bytes, a NUL in the first 8000; or its PATH's diff attribute, a `-diff` line or the `binary` macro in .gitattributes, .git/info/attributes or the file core.attributesFile names, or a driver whose `diff.<driver>.binary` is true), and refuse_hidden_paths read check-attr's VALUE for every regular file at the tip and in each new commit (`unset` hides, a driver hides when its binary key reads true, `set` and `unspecified` do not), so a text file under such an attribute was refused rather than read, and a REAL push through the installed hook had published a synthetic denylist string in one at the r2 text. Round r3 found two ways past that enumeration, each reproduced by real pushes with the denylist armed and gitleaks reporting clean, independently by both refuters and again by the round's fixer: core.bigFileThreshold, a third way git calls a blob binary, in the diff and not in the grep (the hook's own credential half names the key and neutralises it with `--text`; the identifier half had not carried it across), so a text blob over the key printed `Binary files ... differ` in the per-commit read while check-attr answered `unspecified` and the check passed it; and a diff driver NAMED `set` or `unspecified`, with `diff.<driver>.binary` true, for which check-attr answers exactly that word, which the value arm read as no attribute. The ruling (rulings-r3, section A): judge by GIT'S OWN VERDICT, do not neutralise a key and do not parse a value; a `-c core.bigFileThreshold=<large>` on the per-commit read was rejected as a third per-key patch on a privacy surface after two rounds had found the same class, a `--text` on that read was rejected by execution (it reverses the documented binary-by-bytes pass), and the corrections a size-reading route needed (strictly greater than the effective threshold; the unset key falling back to git's built-in default and not to 0; its own cat-file read) were all corrections to reasoning about the cause, the signal to key on the effect; the finding's own fix for the reserved words (reading diff.<value>.binary for them) was rejected too, since it falsely refuses a clean push where `unspecified` is also the answer for a path with no attribute. THE MECHANISM as committed (rounds r3 to r3f, every read of it through judged_read since r6; refuse_hidden_paths, with the helpers as_lines, numstat_rows and combined_rows, and since r6 the joins as named functions the helper runs whole, tip_unlisted_read_file, tip_blobs, tip_candidates, merge_rename_candidates, unlisted_verdict_path, join_candidates and byte_counts, and since r7 nul_records, the shell's count of the rows a join appended): the per-commit half is judged by git's own verdict on the read the scan makes. The population is every non-deleted post-image whose object is a BLOB (a regular file or a symlink, modes 100644, 100755 and 120000; a gitlink names a commit), a type change judged as its new object (the diff prints it as a deletion and an addition judged alone: a numstat of the empty tree against the commit's tree for that path, the tree's name from git hash-object; pinned since r5 by cases 102 and 103, the new link GONE at the tip, where the r3g cases kept it at the tip and the tip-owns rule decided them), less a post-image whose blob equals every pre-image blob (a mode-only change: the diff prints its mode lines with no hunk and no Binary line, so it hid nothing new, while the pair's stat calls an unchanged blob over the key or holding a NUL binary; pinned since r5 by case 106, the change with the file GONE at the tip through a real push, since case 105 keeps the blob at the tip where the tip-owns rule decides it: the property is the blob's absence from the pushed tip, not the file's), and less a blob the pushed TIP's tree holds under any path, which the tip half judges (read by its grep or its symlink pass, or refused as hidden there), so the per-commit road covers only content ABSENT from the tip, the intermediate-commit shape, which bounds the exposure. For a commit with ONE parent, `git diff-tree --numstat` over the pair the per-commit diff reads (-M): a post-image git reports as binary, a dash for each count, is one the diff printed no hunk for, the same decision on the same two blobs (they agree on every blob with bytes; an empty blob under such a rule is the one pair the numstat calls binary while the diff prints its header alone, a candidate here that passes as empty); a PURE rename, which the diff prints nothing for, is judged as the addition of its new path by a second numstat (--no-renames), the rule the r2e rename refusal set, so the explicit diff line the advice names lifts it for the new path (cases 90 and 93: the remedy passes; a file under -diff renamed to a plain path AND changed in the same commit is refused, the diff having judged the pair by the path it came from and printed no hunk; the tip holds the renamed blob in case 90, so the tip-owns rule decides it there, and the exception itself is pinned since r5 by cases 91 and 92, the lifted rename with the new path GONE at the tip passing and its twin with no later diff line for the new path refused, red under m-rename-exception: the property pinned is the blob's absence from the pushed tip, not the file's). For a MERGE, the combined patch itself (`git diff-tree -p -r -M -c --root --no-commit-id --no-color`, the read added_lines makes, byte for byte between the commit's sha lines that added_lines asks for with --always since r8 and a second --stdin line since r8b3), read again and parsed section by section (combined_rows, the header's path C-unquoted as git quotes it): a section printing `Binary files differ` is a path the patch printed no hunk for, a candidate; a section with a hunk was read; a path with the header alone is a candidate judged by its bytes (an added empty file passes as empty; a non-empty blob so printed would be refused as hidden, an arm no shape found reaches, since a header-only section for a blob with bytes is a mode change over content some parent holds); a path with no section whose blob some parent holds under a path the merge lacks is a pure rename, judged as the addition of its new path by the empty-tree numstat, the candidates read from the merge's deletions against EACH parent (`diff-tree --raw -m`, since the combined listing names a deletion only when every parent held the path); a path with no section that is no rename is a verdict the read owed and did not print, refused as unscanned. The combined patch judges by a rule of its own that no per-parent verdict reproduces: it applies no size rule, and it applies a path's diff attribute to a symlink as the pairwise diff does not, which is why r3d replaced the per-parent numstat approximation for merges (r3's disclosed excess, a merge's own change to a file over the key refused as hidden while the patch had printed its hunk, went with it). The tip half likewise derives the set git's grep treats as binary from git's own report: the same `git grep -I`, asked to list the non-empty files it READ (`-l -z -e ''`), the complement over the tree's regular files with bytes (`ls-tree -r -z -l`) being the files it skipped; a symlink's target is read whole by the symlink pass, by cat-file, whatever any attribute or key says. No attribute value is parsed anywhere for a decision: check-attr is read once per REFUSED path for the report's label alone (`diff.<value>.binary` looked up first for every answer, since a driver may be named `set`, `unspecified` or `unset`; then `unset` names the -diff line or macro; any other answer names no attribute, and the line states the blob's size and the clone's core.bigFileThreshold as facts a reader needs and not as a cause, or, for a one-parent commit's changed file whose previous version is binary by its bytes, that cause, the key's facts beside it where the key is set), and a check-attr that fails, answers about another path or answers nothing changes the words and never the verdict (since r5b the tail as well: a failed read says the attribute could not be read and that whether an attribute of its path accounts for the verdict is unknown, both facts kept and both remedies printed, where the r5 text asserted after a failed read what the read could not establish). Two SCOPE constraints, the ruling's, both by execution: per-commit ONLY, since `git grep -I` on the tip is unaffected by the size key and a tip-side size or binary rule would be a FALSE refusal (case 97: a big text file carrying the string AT the tip under the key is a HIT by the tip grep, which reads it, and the tip is never called hidden); and an explicit `diff` attribute keeps outranking the key, in the grep and in the diff alike, which still prints the hunk under the key, so it is a hit and never a refusal (case 96), and it is the remedy the advice names. Each blob so named is then read (`git cat-file blob`, the first 8000 bytes counted with od and awk, the rest drained) and judged by git's own byte rule with no diff machinery: no bytes (an empty blob hides nothing) or a NUL (binary, which the scan never read) passes as before, the documented pass (a binary file's string, contract shape c6, byte-identical on every text), and anything else is text the scan could not see: one line per place and path, `romp pre-push: <path> at the tip of <ref> (<sha10>) is text that its diff attribute (<unset, or diff=<driver>, binary>) hides from the identifier scan, so the push is refused rather than scanned`, or `<path> in commit <sha10> ...` for a new commit's post-image (the r2c lines, byte for byte), or, where no attribute accounts for the verdict, `... is text that git calls binary although its diff attribute reads <value>, so no attribute of its path accounts for the verdict (the blob is <n> bytes; core.bigFileThreshold is <value, or not set> in this clone's configuration); the identifier scan did not read it, so the push is refused rather than scanned` (a one-parent commit's symlink says instead that it is a symbolic link judged by its size and bytes and by no attribute of the path; a rename says the earlier path counted too; a changed file whose previous version holds a NUL says that version's bytes are the cause, and where the key is set adds the key's facts), hidden_paths=1 and failed_scan=1, and, after the ref loop, the advice: the two r2e lines naming the attribute as the cause with the explicit-diff remedy and the rename clause (byte for byte; since r5b printed only where a refused line named the attribute as the cause or could not read it, hidden_attr, where the r5 text printed them on every hidden refusal, the disclosed shape's included, so the first remedy the pusher read was for a cause every line said did not account for the verdict), since r3 a third line where a line named no attribute (hidden_unnamed: core.bigFileThreshold as the one key git has today, its value and the blob's size shown, raise it above the size or unset it and push again, and since r5b in its own words the explicit "<path> diff" line, which outranks the key as well for a regular file, since the attribute paragraph is no longer there to point at, while a one-parent commit's diff applies no attribute to a symbolic link's target, so for such a link the key is the remedy), and since r3e a fourth where a line named the previous version's bytes (hidden_prior: the hook scans only commits new to every fetched remote, so fetch the remote that holds the commit first and push again, or push from a clone that has; a new commit of the shape is refused the same way, since the scan cannot read its new text, and since r6b has a second line naming an explicit diff attribute on the PREVIOUS version's path, the old path for a rename and the file's own path otherwise, under which git treats that version as text whatever its bytes, so the diff prints the change and the scan reads it, named ahead of the bypass it is safer than; the hidden_attr paragraph stays withheld there, the gate's prior clause kept; extra5-4 and tests-1, cases 187 to 189, and 110, 113 and 114 asserting the line). Every read the check makes fails closed through unscanned naming the read (the scratch directory, the tip's listing and its read-list grep, each commit's listing, its two numstats, a merge's combined patch and its per-parent listing, the empty tree's name and the numstat against it, a cat-file, since r5 the byte judge's size read and since r5b the added lines' own scratch directory; since r6 each through judged_read with its expected set, the joins' awk and tr, combined_rows' awk and as_lines' tr and wc included, where a tool that could not run ended the hook by errexit with no line): the STATUS-KEYED class; and since r5 the EXIT-0 EMPTY-OR-SHORT-ANSWER class is gated as a class (round 4's ruling), each of the check's reads tagged gate= or own= at its call since r6 (round 5's ruling: the header's lists derived from the tags): a numstat or patch that exits 0 and answers for fewer paths than the commit changes is a SHORT READ refused as unscanned (ruling B's class, gated on every read having exited 0 so one cause prints and not two; since r5b naming the first path met no verdict), a commit's changed-path listing must name every path its numstats or its combined patch answer for (a listing that exits 0 with nothing, on a one-parent commit or on a merge, refused as listed short naming the listing and the path; the merge decided by the parent count the caller read, never by the listing's shape), the tip's -z listing must agree with the symlink pass's entry count and name every file the grep lists as read (refused as listed short or long naming both counts, or naming the file), each scratch listing's rewrite must hold as many bytes as its input with every count a run of digits (refused naming the rewrite and both answers), and a blob read as zero bytes passes as empty only when cat-file -s gives its size as 0 (refused naming the read and the size); a path holding a newline byte is refused as unscanned rather than judged by nothing; and whatever was listed is still judged. Decided by execution at the r3g text, each by a REAL push with a shim exiting 0 and answering nothing or short: the listing (cases 153, 154), the tip's listing (139, 140, 141), the rewrite (160 to 163) and the blob read (165, 166) each PUBLISHED a hidden text file or passed the push with nothing printed, and each is refused since r5; the reads whose empty answer is the object's own are the header's own= list, derived from the call sites and pinned by case 174, and since r8b generated from the table's own= rows and pinned by the table case (the property paragraph above; the residual paragraph names the three joins among the ten at r7b and the eight since r8b3; and since r7 the tip candidates join and the join of a commit's verdicts are gate=, the first against the -z listing's regular files with bytes less the grep's read list, the second against the count its awk recorded, each shown publishing by a real push with a silent awk and a silent tr at the r6b text, hook cases 195 to 198). The r2c flags ruled in r2e stand as recorded (a hidden text file at the tip and in a new commit named once per place, since r3d the tip's line alone where the tip holds the blob; an EMPTY blob passes; the pure rename refused on the safe side with the advice that earns it; the explicit `rm` by the exact minted path with the residual at the header). One shape is refused on purpose and DISCLOSED at the header with its remedy, not an open road: a commit that turns a file binary by its bytes into a TEXT file prints `Binary files differ` for the pair (either side binary makes the pair binary, whatever the key says), so the new text was read by neither check and the commit is refused as hidden, its line naming the previous version's bytes as the cause: since r5 for a one-parent commit's change or rename (the version in its parent, at the path the diff read it from) and for a merge whose result is such a text file while a parent's version is binary (the combined patch prints Binary for it too; that parent's version named), so every refusal of the class names its cause and the fetch remedy and no key advice, where until r5 the merge twin and the rename twin were refused with the two-fact line and the key advice, the cause unnamed (the r3e fixer's FLAG 1, ruled in round 4 as extra9-1 and extra9-2); three commits in this repository's own history have that shape (a source file that once held a raw NUL byte, replaced by a text version), a clone that has fetched any remote excludes them from every range, and a clone with NO remote-tracking refs pushing the history as a new ref is refused on them, so the remedy is to fetch first, or to push from a clone that has (found by the r3d fixer's whole-history run and confirmed exact by the r3e audit's, 2026-09-22; cases 110 to 114). Verified by execution at every text, each round's fixer and auditor apart: the r2c recipe still refused with the remote holding nothing; the r3 shapes by real pushes (16 of 16 at the r3 text, five PUBLISHED at 89589e7d3); the r3 audit's 36 refute rows; the r3c audit's 59-row matrix of every post-image kind under every rule tried, 59 of 59 as the property predicts; the r3d audit's 42 merge rows; the r3e and r3f audits' probes (the class paragraph above). THE SHAPES THAT PUBLISHED OR PASSED, named one by one because they are the argument that the identifier half needed closing in this fold and then closing again by derivation (romp-manager's condition; each verified by the round's fails-before and by a real push in a scratch clone; bats case numbers at the round's text, then at the committed text): (1) a text file under a `-diff` attribute in the tree's .gitattributes, carrying a denylist string, pushed with the denylist armed: PUBLISHED at b42231339 (the identifier scan's grep skipped it as binary and its added-lines diff printed no line); refused since e40a82da0, naming the path and the attribute, the remote holding nothing (case 71 at r2c, now 82). (7) A text file under a diff driver NAMED `set`, with `diff.set.binary` true, carrying the string, pushed with the denylist armed, and the same under a driver named `unspecified`: PUBLISHED at 89589e7d3 (check-attr answered the reserved word, which the value arm read as no attribute, and both content reads skipped the file; the r3 fixer's rows d1 and d2); refused since 29bc29891, the line naming the driver (`diff=set, binary`; `diff=unspecified, binary`), the remote holding nothing (cases 94 and 95 at r3, now 132 and 133), with the control the refuters asked for green at both texts: a driver merely named `unspecified`, with no attribute naming it on any path, hides nothing, and a clean push passes with nothing printed (case 96 at r3, now 134). Beside them, the two shapes the round r3 and r3c auditors found PUBLISHED at the derivation's first scope, each closed in the round that followed: (8) a SYMLINK whose target is over core.bigFileThreshold and carries the string, added in a middle commit and gone at the tip (the size key reaches a symlink's target blob in the per-commit diff, which prints `Binary files /dev/null and b/link differ` and no target, while no attribute does, and the tip's symlink pass reads every target by cat-file): PUBLISHED at 29bc29891 and at 89589e7d3 (the r3 audit's row s12: a real push with the key at 20 and a 124-byte target; the r3 population was regular files alone); refused since def7b95d1, the line naming the commit and the link (case 94 at r3c, now 98). (9) A MERGE's own symlink at a path under a `-diff` attribute, the string in its target, gone at the tip (the combined patch applies the attribute to a symlink and prints `Binary files differ`, where the pairwise diff, and so the per-parent numstat, print the target): PUBLISHED at def7b95d1 and at every earlier text (the r3c fixer's row m6, disclosed at that text as an open road with its witness, the deferred-fix condition met in one home of three until r3d); refused since f45ff1a11 through a real push, the line naming the merge, the link and its attribute (case 105 at r3d, now 115), and with it three roads of the same class the r3d audit found published at def7b95d1 (a merge's own symlink under the binary macro, an octopus merge's symlink under -diff, a merge changing a link's target under -diff), each refused since. Beside the nine, at the r2c text, two reads the r2 text did not make (r2c's 79 and 80, re-aimed in r3 to the label read and the cat-file, now 168 and 164), since r2e the pure rename (82, now 89), since r3 the arms' cases (135 to 138, 142 to 152 and 155 to 159) and the short-read gate (144), since r5 the second class's cases (139 to 141, 153, 154, 160 to 163 and 165 to 167) and the arms' pins off the tip (91, 92, 102, 103 and 106), since r5b the NUL-bearing added line (5, 6), the quoted paths (128, 129) and the octopus pair (130, 131); the contract runs byte-identical at every round (16 of 16; 17 of 18, the null-byte warning's line); the fork's own tip through the same reads, at 7899359a5, the head the r6b round checked it at (the counts are in the round records, since they move at every upkeep merge): none hidden, every regular file the grep skipped binary by its bytes (the tree's one .gitattributes marks a fixture `-text` only; no .git/info/attributes; core.attributesFile and core.bigFileThreshold unset; every diff attribute unspecified); the check's rows are 1578 to 2115 in the population above (1554 to 2091 at the r9b text, 1520 to 2056 at the r8c text, 1164 to 1678 at the r7b text).

The method is the deliverable, not the count of roads: the population is what the two commands list PLUS execution at every site a command tags as a status read of an external binary (a read inside that binary is visible only as the binary's invocation, and only execution showed that its status does not carry the read), PLUS, since r1, the less-than-source question asked of every row (a read loop's head, a substitution's strip, a filter's cut, a stream transformed by the clone's own configuration before either reader sees it). The method's limits, for the next reader: the commands see statements, not reach (errexit closes a substitution only outside a condition context; the b tag excludes if and while heads, so each b row is judged by context); a helper's pipeline shows unclosed at the helper and closed at its caller; a count derived from the same stream as the scan is only as independent as the stream is clean, which is why r1 had both reads ask git for a plain stream, why the r1 derivation was run over the fixer's text before it was committed, and why r2 took the count off the stream altogether (a plain-stream option closes the transform it names; an object read closes the class), and why r2c, where the identifier arm's reads skip a blob on its path's attribute, refuses rather than adds a read (a scan that cannot see a blob cannot call it clean; reading it from the objects is the longer fix the round named and left); and why r2e re-ran a premise the entry, the hook's header and a bats comment had each recorded as a fact (that no option reaches log.showRoot) and found it false: a claim that a transform has no option is a claim about git, settled by running the option, not by the derivation's tags, and two rounds and a ruling had built on it unrun; and why r3 replaced an ENUMERATION with a derivation: the r2c closure listed the ways git calls a blob binary and closed the road by each attribute value, and the list was short by one key and two reserved words that real pushes found (the same class round r2 had ruled on for the credential half, a guard keyed on a name, a spelling or a value rather than on the property), so the identifier half now asks each read for its own verdict, and rounds r3c to r3f then asked, by real pushes over every post-image kind and merge shape the auditors could build, whether the derivation's scope equals the property's, the question a derived property owes and an enumeration cannot answer, and found and closed four gaps in it before the answer was yes; and why round 4 had the exit-0 class gated as a CLASS (every read whose empty or short answer would be read as nothing to judge, checked against a second read in hand) where round 3 had gated the numstat and check-attr alone, and had every arm's pin put its blob off the tip: a guarantee scoped to statuses left every exit-0 read a road of its own, four found by real pushes in one round, and a case the tip-owns rule decides is a pin that cannot red; the tags are the first cut and the disposition column above is the verdict; and why round 8 made the population a predicate: rounds 5 to 7 found the class open one site at a time because the hook's reads were listed by tag and not judged by a rule, so A.8's table judges every read by the fact that bounds its answer, with a case that drives it through a real push, and rounds r8b to r8b5 found by driving it that a fact bounding an answer's START (a head marker, a non-empty answer, an emptiness compared with a size) says nothing of its END, so every row now states both and a case cuts each read short, or the row says why no cut applies. Since then the two commands are the record of the statements and the table's rule is the verdict. Round 9 found the last limit of both: a read made inside another tool is visible to neither the commands nor the table, which is why round r9d took the read inside gitleaks into the hook's own feed, where the table judges it, rather than judge gitleaks' log of a read the hook cannot wrap (item 33).

A residual of a separate class, recorded here so the follow-up can take it, and re-read in rounds r7c, r8c, r9bc, r9e, r10c and r11d against the committed hook (the numbers below the r9d text's; the additions of rounds r10c and r11d cite by function). The nine JUDGE sites of the r5b text (its 1300, 1301, 1343, 1352, 1391, 1409, 1422, 1451 and 1460; 1079, 1080, 1118, 1127, 1155, 1170, 1183, 1212 and 1221 on the r3g text) were greps over captured text that branched the same way on no-match (status 1) and on a grep that could not run (status 2, or 127 off PATH), shown by execution with a grep shim exiting 2 ahead of the real one, a leaking tip, the denylist armed: ten `grep: simulated failure` lines and hook exit 0, where the real-grep control refused. CLOSED in r6 by the construction, not by the startup self-test this paragraph had proposed: every grep over captured content runs through judged_read with the expected set 0 and 1, so a status of 2 or 127 is refused as unscanned naming the read (the added-lines grep at 2616, the symlink-target grep at 2515, the address-domain greps at 2680 and 2818, the chosen-address match at the r7b text's 579 until r8, when is_chosen stopped running a tool, the message grep at 2148 for a commit and for a tag; each shown by a real push with a grep shim exiting 2: hook cases 175 and 176, identity 37 to 39, message 19 and 20, every one red at the r5b text), and the r5b text's two filters over the tip grep's output (its 1300 and 1301) and its two greps over the symlink listing (its 1342 and 1343) are bash loops now (2368-2375, 2468-2476), with no tool to fail. What STANDS on the committed text, each with its witness: (1) the second class's BOUND, the seven own= reads since r9d (eight from r8b3 to r9b, ten at r7b), whose empty answer is declared the object's own at the call site and so outside the guarantee (the witness: the header's derived list, and case 174, which pins that the list is the tags and nothing else, red under four header mutants and, since r7b, red with the chosen-addresses tag removed from the list; since r8b the table, which gives each own= read the reason an empty or cut-short answer there refuses or publishes nothing and a case for each drive, pinned by the table case), stated at the header since r7 as the DERIVED BOUND with why a tool silent at each publishes nothing by itself: the ADDRESS DOMAIN grep (2680 and 2818) and the SYMLINK TARGET grep (2515), two -q greps whose answer is their status alone (exit 0 is the hit, so no line is no answer withheld; and until r9d the RANGE probe of the credential scan (the r9b text's 3057), whose status alone was read, the answer discarded, retired with the scan it served. The residual the bound leaves outside the guarantee, named at the header: two tools silent at once, a listing and its gate (a -z listing short AND the join that checks it silent; a commit's listing short AND its verdict join silent); since r8b4 the same bound stated for the scratch listing's two byte counts, the rewrite and its counts cut at once being outside the guarantee as a listing and its gate are; and, the r7 hook fixer's FLAG 2 and the r7 audit's note 2, that own= reads still run a tool (four at r7b, the three joins and the ERR line's awk; three since r8b3, the joins, the ERR line being gate=) where ruling A's closing sentence reads "no external stage": the paragraph states the bound per read instead, a description and not a requirement in the fixer's and the auditor's reading, romp-manager's question; the rewrite in the shell (set membership over the scratch listings with associative arrays, a `read -r` loop over the log, about forty lines) is the other option, recoverable. (2) The ten reads declared outside the helper since r9d (twelve from r7b to r9b) (`outside judged_read:`), each with its reason at its site: the eight report reads inside the refused line (2003 the label's check-attr, 2012 the driver key, 2029 the blob's size, 2030 the threshold key, 2074 the parent count's awk over the hook's own pcount file, a read of no object, 2079 the rename listing and 2087 and 2093 the previous version's bytes in a parent and in each parent of a merge), which decide nothing (cases 168 to 170 and 187: a failed check-attr leaves the refusal standing with the tail open and both remedies printed); until r9d the one configuration read whose swallowed failure was the strict side (the r9b text's 800, log.showRoot, read after the ref list since r8: case 173, and case 210 for a git that drains stdin on it; retired in r9d with the scan it served, both cases re-aimed; the chosen-addresses read left this class in r7b, case 172 having shown its swallowed failure PUBLISH a clean-domain commit the read should have refused as unscanned); the ref list on stdin (817 to 819: the shell's own read since r7, no tool on PATH between git and the loops, a read error ending the hook under set -u, refusing the push; case 200); the scanner's own exit (3108 in scanner_run since r9d, the r9b text's 3091: judged with its log against the bytes the feed wrote, the eighth road's remedy, whole at the granularity of bytes since r9d); and until r9d the root-commit probe of the advice arm (the r9b text's 3163: a line added to a refusal already made), retired with the arm. Case 203 pins since r7b that no read stands outside the helper undeclared beyond the declared ones (the marker count 10 lines at the r7b hook by the census's reading, the same at the r8b5, r8c and r9b hooks, and 8 at the r9d hook, the two retired declarations gone, since the ref list's and the scanner's declarations carry no tool word; 14 marker lines by grep at r7b, 16 at r8b5, at r8c and at r9b and 14 at r9d, the header's sentence, a mention in the generated block and one in the scratch paragraph, and a block declaration over the report's reads beside the declared reads; the two shapes the r7b census did not read, a backtick substitution and a second read after a call's `--`, it reads since r8b under ruling B, and since r9b and round r9bc it reads more shapes still, pins others ABSENT from the hook's comment-stripped raw text and discloses shapes it neither reads nor pins, each with a witness in case 203 that passes both, the hook holding none (ruling F; five at round r9bc, a list found shape by shape, and since round r10c eight, derived from bash's expansions of a command word, ruling J; the property paragraph above)), and since r8b each declared read that runs a tool the census reads has an outside row in tests/pre-push-reads.tsv, keyed to its command line, eight since r9d (ten until then, beside the scanner's argument list, whose row r9d retired). (3) The scratch directory left behind by a hard kill between its minting and its removal, since r8 by the EXIT trap (row 847; stated at the header; 0 leftovers in every round's runs under an exclusive TMPDIR), and beside it since r8 extra5-8's shape (ruling G): the directory's writers stand outside judged_read's guard, so a scratch directory the hook cannot write into ends the hook at its first write under set -e with bash's own line and no romp line, in default mode and under POSIXLY_CORRECT alike, every arm refusing the push (the round 7 refuter's six probes; case 212, which also shows the directory removed), and no guarded writer is added. (4) The disclosed binary-to-text refusal with its two remedies (the identifier-half paragraph above: a refusal stated, not an open road; cases 110 to 114 and 186 to 189). (5) One shape outside every case and outside the rulings, recorded by the r7 rounds and standing: a pusher's environment exporting POSIXLY_CORRECT makes a failed `exec 9>file` end the hook with bash's own line and no romp line, still refused, without the name (the r7 hook fixer's FLAG 6). The two others the r7 rounds recorded are closed: under a failed chosen-addresses read the remedy paragraph under BLOCKED leads with the failed read and its remedy and withholds the say-so line and its bullets, and a tag refused on its tagger address withholds its re-create remedy the same way (ruling C, flag 80, round r8b; cases 171 and 207 re-aimed); and the false-cause line for a refused CHOSEN ADDRESS match has no road, since is_chosen runs no tool (ruling C, flag 82, round r8; identity case 39 re-aimed at the true cause, 41 and 42 new). (6) The closures of rounds r8b to r8b5, found by the fold owner's own rounds after round 7's ruling, the ERR line and the chosen addresses among them, the two own= reads whose rows said safe (the first silent, the second cut short; ruling H.1 of round 8), with round 9's amendments of them (the loop census, the ancestry's peel, the rows' text): the proposal pending the repository owner's decision under A.8's last sentence until round 9, when the owner took option (b) for the read inside gitleaks and flags 87, 88 and 89, which carry those closures, were accepted with it (the class statement above), so the closures stand as built and the read inside gitleaks, made the hook's own, has its row (the property paragraph above; item 33). (7) A valid tip whose tree holds only empty directories, which git's own index never writes: the recursive listing omits tree entries and answers empty while the tree's size is not 0, so the tip is refused, its line naming both causes, a listing cut short or such a tree (ruling D, round r9a; stated in the table's rows for the SYMLINK listing and the SIZE of the tip's tree and so in the generated header; case 361, a one-entry tip built with git mktree and git commit-tree, pushed for real). (8) Since r9d, stated at the header and pinned (item 33): gitleaks' built-in path exemptions do not reach the hook's scan, since the pieces carry no real path (case 387), a false alarm excused in .gitleaks.toml by exact value; a match with more than 16,384 of its bytes before a piece boundary, in a file whose added lines pass 98,304 bytes or in a line longer than 81,916 bytes, is whole in neither piece (so stated since round r10b, ruling C, where this text had said a match longer than 16,384 bytes; cases 435 and 437 witness the first loss, published); a piece whose bytes carry a binary type's signature at its fixed offset is skipped by gitleaks and refused by the byte figure, loudly, never published; a repository config that drops one of the five path-scoped rules refuses every push that adds a file those rules name (the r9d hook fixer's FLAG 3); and the byte figure is a log line gitleaks does not document, so a gitleaks that stops writing it refuses every push with content, visibly, ROMP_NO_GITLEAKS=1 skipping one push. (9) Since round 10, stated at the header and pinned (item 34): a merge's binary path read whole from its result blob, so a merge whose binary path's whole blob holds a credential a remote already published refuses the push (its witness the round 10b case titled "round 10b (A.2's disclosed cost, with its witness)"); a commit of 64 or more parents refused in both scans, since git's combined diff drops or garbles the lines it adds (cases 411 and 412); gitleaks 8.25.0 or later required, a push with something to scan refused under an older one or one whose version answer is not a dotted version (cases 442 and 444; case 443, at the floor, proceeds); and the copies' directories made by mkdir in bounded batches until round 11c, scratch writers under residual (3), where a batch that failed ended the hook under set -e with bash's line and no romp line. (10) Since round 11, stated at the header and pinned (item 35): a repository config whose rules carry a path condition refuses the push where a scan would run, before either scanner run, naming the rule and the file, exact support for path-conditioned rules a held follow-up; exempt are one of the five path-scoped rules whose path text is byte-equal to the hook's own table entry for its id (tl_default_path) and an empty path in any of TOML's four quote forms, and a third [extend] level is refused visibly, a rare shape; a per-rule or targeted path allowlist, in either condition, or a rule's own path, that treats a copy's name differently from the file's path changes that rule's verdict in the copy without skipping it, so the figure agrees, and the probe run and the second copy refuse it, at the cost that a push adding a selected file refuses when the config drops that file's rule at the copy's name, clean or not (the round 11b case titled "round 11b (A, the cost witness)"), and also where the file's path gets the same verdict (the round 11b case titled "round 11b (A, the same-verdict witness)"), every push with a selected file paying one more scanner run; a global path allowlist that matches a copy's name skips it and refuses on the figure, never publishing; the probe line claims no path allowlist, since a value allowlist, a stopword or a raised entropy floor can drop a probe too, the probes' values chosen so that only the repository's own allowlists can drop them (the coordinator's decision 1); the AND residual: an AND-condition allowlist whose path matches both the copy's name and the second copy's name, and not the file's path, with value criteria that match the credential, publishes it; such a path keys on the synthetic stem or layout and has no honest use, and the value it publishes is one the config excuses by value (the round 11b case titled "round 11b (A, the residual, OUTSIDE the closed class)"); and the per-piece directories made by xargs (piece_dirs), a writer judged as a read, so a failure there refuses with a romp line and no longer falls under residual (3). Nothing else of the judge or the tool-failure class remains on the committed text.

tests/pre-push-message.bats), and since r1 a second, `git_passing_through`, that runs the real git and for one shape first writes a stderr line or appends an argument, keyed on gitleaks' own invocation shape (gitleaks_git_noisy, gitleaks_git_short, gitleaks_git_warning), and since r2 scan_direct reads gitleaks_args out of the hook and the transforms section's helpers hiding_textconv, signed_commit and substitute_for build each setting, and since r2c the gate section's path_without_gitleaks (every PATH directory holding a gitleaks replaced by a shadow of symlinks to its other entries, so `command -v gitleaks` finds nothing and the hook's other tools still resolve) and the attribute section's push_main_through_hook, remote_holds_main and attributes (one real push of main through the installed hook to a bare remote, the remote's state asserted), and since r2e and r2h the showRoot section's scan_direct_without_root (the hook's own argument list with `--root` taken out of its log options, pinning first that the list carries it: the road as git applies the key), gitleaks_git_recording_pairs (a git that records the command-scope configuration the scanner's git sees, for the negative pin that the hook exports no pair), gitleaks_without_config_pairs (a scanner wrapper stripping every GIT_CONFIG_COUNT, GIT_CONFIG_KEY_n and GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_n variable, the shape an environment override could not reach, kept to show the option does) and gitleaks_without_root_option (a wrapper removing the word `--root` from the scanner's log options and keeping every other, recording the list it ran: the one way the arm can fire for this key now), and since r3 the derivation case's git_stripping_root_from_log (a git shim stripping `--root` from every invocation carrying the word `log` and from no diff-tree, so the scanner's log loses the root's hunk while the hook's object reads keep the root's entry), the arms' shims in the file's existing idiom, each keyed on the one read it refuses (fail_for_each_ref; fail_ls_tree_z, keyed on `-z` present so the symlink pass's plain ls-tree stands and tip_listed stays set; fail_grep_read_list, keyed on the read list's `-z`, the content grep carrying none; fail_diff_tree_raw, keyed on `--raw`, the r3 audit's repair; fail_diff_tree_numstat and empty_diff_tree_numstat, keyed on `--numstat`, the second exiting 0 with nothing; fail_hash_object; fail_empty_tree_numstat and empty_empty_tree_numstat, keyed on the :(literal) pathspecs; fail_combined_patch and empty_combined_patch, keyed on `-p -c`; fail_per_parent_listing and empty_per_parent_listing, keyed on `--raw -m`; and check_attr_answering, a git whose check-attr prints a given answer and exits 0, the real git for every other command), the fixtures big_text_file (a text file over a threshold of 100), long_target and symlink_commit (a committed link whose target is over a threshold of 20), type_change_to_symlink, merge_fixture and merge_with_hidden_link (a merge left open for the case's own change), third_path_merge and third_path_committed (parents holding the content under two paths, the merge under a third), push_main_through_installed_hook (a first push without the hook, then the change through it, so a mode-only change reaches the hook alone), and since r2f fail_config_user_email, commit_stamped_as and fail_config_showroot (the two config reads' failures), since r5 the second class's shims, empty_ls_tree_z (the verdict check's -z listing answering nothing with a clean status, the symlink pass's plain ls-tree untouched), ls_tree_z_dropping (a listing without one path's record), empty_diff_tree_raw_c (the combined changed-path listing alone, `--raw` and `-c` both present), tr_silent_on (a tr whose rewrite of an input holding a marker exits 0 and writes nothing), wc_silent (a wc answering nothing with exit 0), silent_cat_file_blob (a `cat-file blob` exiting 0 and printing nothing for one blob, the size read untouched) and od_failing (an od reading its input and exiting 1), with the fixtures hidden_file_on_remote_then_clean_commit (a -diff file carrying the string on the remote, then The upstream shape: one offer covering the class, the fixed hook with its nine closed roads and the class behind the ninth closed structurally (until r9d the object derivation and gitleaks_args with its ten plain-stream options, and since r9d the hook's own read of the pushed changes, the credential feed, handed to gitleaks as exactly those bytes and judged by its byte figure, since r10a every line of it read, recognized or routed and any other refused, a merge's binary path read whole from its result blob and a commit of 64 or more parents refused, and since r10b gitleaks 8.25.0 or later required, since r11a a repository config whose rules carry a path condition refused by the hook's own reader, and since r11b every run handed the reader's copy and a probe run over each copy's name and a second copy of each selected piece; and the two classes of fail-open the guarantee covers, closed by construction, the second's bound the derived own= list) as parts of the one offer, rather than listing the instances found, so the project does not stop looking at the six;
